- Milestone 3 (planner): Cache-aware estimates in `pricing.ts`, once Stage 4 has measured the read shares per role.
- Milestone 3 (planner): On adoption: move the message shaping to `shared/llm/` beside the factory, and decide an explicit cache TTL there (a production request-shape change, the owner's call).
- Milestone 3 (planner): A Luna low rewrite arm, the next cheaper turn arm (about $0.1).
- Milestone 3 (review): **Price the JSON schema at its real density in the estimates** (about 1.8 characters per token, not 4), or borrow measured input tokens along the reference chain as `measuredFor` does for outputs. Setup estimates read 28–32% low on input today. This changes every stage's estimates and the runner's reservations, so it wants its own change.
- Milestone 3 (eval run): **An eval-only output cap for replays** (`max_completion_tokens` on `--run`, recorded on the call). The runner has none, so a hung case can't be replayed to see where a loop starts.
- Setup rounds: **Before round 1 is adopted for templates**, the template editor's outcome editor must show and edit contested outcomes (setup doc B1.11): round 1 makes them common in competitive templates. And a widened single-player template has no shared outcome for the first grouped thread (B1.16). Neither blocks the eval run.
- Setup rounds: **Score the two open setup pages** when the owner's exports arrive: the final page (`--score` on `ratings-text-setup-bc2117291e.json`) and the templates page (`ratings-text-setup-4363b79c75.json`). Pairwise pages have no ranks, so `--check-baselines --ratings` leaves them out.
- Setup rounds, round 3: **Single-player seats with three identical names, what is left:** round 3's identity clause and `distinctIdentities` brought them from 7 to 2 of 36 setups (a shed setup "Rowan" ×3, a cancellation setup "Alex Moreno" ×3; today's prompt 0). Read those two premises for a role word the clause's "not only a role" misses before adoption.
- Setup rounds, round 3: **At adoption of the setup form, one clause against per-player stats** (Casablanca wrote "Fatima's …"/"Layla's …" player stats in both samples, round 2b 0 of 2): the multiplayer identity clause says "use those names in outcomes and stats"; make it "in outcomes" and keep round 1c's "every player gets every player stat". Untested (the brief allowed one confirmation); rerun the Casablanca premise ×2 with it.
- Setup rounds, round 3: **At play adoption, repair an opposites scoreboard written as a string:** a switch turn wrote `setString "35|65"` on the bounty hunters' opposites scoreboard, which `ChangeService` drops, so a won contest moved nothing. In `beatRepairs.ts`, turn a `setString "a|b"` on an opposites stat whose parts are numbers summing to 100 into `setNumber a`, with a repair count; or have the turn's stat-change field say opposites take numbers. Test-first against the stored reply (`outputs/deb990df5ef1004b6712`).
- Setup rounds, round 3: **The kids rule's stat examples are copied:** "Courage" (the first of `KIDS_STATS`' "Courage, Snacks, Forest Friends") names a stat in all four kids confirmation setups, "Forest Friends" in one. Vary or drop the examples at adoption; `noExampleNameLeak` doesn't read the kids line, so add its names there if the examples stay.
- Setup rounds, round 3: **Camps put player1 alone against two in 4 of 4 three-player contest setups** (player1's camp is side A, and every setup gave side A one seat). The owner's call whether a camp of two should sometimes be player1's; the form says nothing about camp sizes.
- Setup rounds, fix run: **`noExampleCopy` reads the adjustments field's own required phrase as a copy** ("after a favorable challenge thread in which the …": 11 of arm B's 36 setups, every one of its flags). Leaving out the eight-word runs that the request's own field descriptions contain would read content copies only; it moves the stored readings of rounds 1 to 2b, so re-render with `--results`.
- Setup rounds: **The word-list checks' false alarms** found in round 1: `identityNames` takes titles and common words from identity names ("Dr." matched an NPC's title, "Bell" a "Buried Bell Seal", "Kit" a "Personal Field Kit"), so `noSlotNames` and `noIdentityNamesInOutcomes` fail on them; `FORMULA` matches a ladder value ("Divided") and a premise's own "per" ("cost per cup"). Skipping honorifics and requiring a capitalised name word next to other name words, and reading `divided` only as an operator, would cut them; each change moves the stored baselines, so re-run `--check-baselines` with it.
- Setup rounds, round 3: **The chapter planner's thread type is still written freely** (turn doc A5), so a change after threads keyed on a type's name can't be matched until that lands: the bounty hunters' chain planned its first chapter as a "challenge" and ran it as a contest on the contested outcome. The chain also has not yet seen an exploration chapter's milestone, a two-camp contest in play, or an ending; a longer chain (`--setup-chain` past the first switch) would read them.
- Setup rounds: **When play adopts the scoreboard ending rule** (decision 3, turn doc B8: "the side ahead on its scoreboard wins unless its milestones clearly say otherwise; 45-55 a draw"), change three round-1 sentences in the same step: A3.1's "one beat per player writes that player's ending from the outcomes' milestones", A1.2's "they are the story's only progress bar", and A4.1's "nothing in the game reads a stat to decide an outcome or to end the story". Each is true of today's code only. The turn doc's section 3.2 lists the A3.1 block's other changes (B2, A4, A6) but not this one.
- Turn rounds: **At planner v2's adoption:** A1's plan check with one retry (the 10-turn story's chapter plan still picked 3 beats in 1 of 2 plans, where the pacing block said 2), and "without a number" in the chapter title field (one multiplayer plan of about 40 titled its chapter "6. …").
- Turn rounds: **"You" paragraphs rise on every new turn form** (22% today; framed 25%, slim 30%, slim retest 27%, the multiplayer chain on the framed turn 36%). The forms share the chapter block and the progress rule, whose good example is in the second person; unmeasured which. Round 2's prose block (B5) targets You openings; read it against this, and try the progress rule's example in the third person if B5 doesn't bring it back.
- Turn round 3: **If B9's gate run is funded, run the round-2 form beside it in the same hour on the same cases** (the server's pace moved from 7.2 to 11 ms per output token between evenings), and count cut attempts and one-paragraph turns as B9's risk readings. If B10 runs, keep "other beats" in the arm that keeps the note alone, and build the consistency judge on the hand read's criterion (the shared lines word for word and the same speaker), calibrated on the 12 turns read by hand here.
- Turn rounds: **The framed turn on multiplayer has only chain readings** (14 Luna low turns). Before any multiplayer adoption, an isolated multiplayer arm on the 12 stored multiplayer cases (turn round 3's B10) would separate the planner's effect from the turn's.

## Round 0 play fixes (implementer)

Unit 1 (turn replies keep what they write, TR-1 to TR-10). Decisions beyond `.plans/2026-09-27_play-fixes-round0.md`:

- **Repair kinds** (`server/src/game/services/beatRepairs.ts`), which unit 5 counts as `repair:<kind>`: `statIdSeatForm`, `statGroupMoved`, `statChangeAmbiguous`, `statChangeUnknown`, `offLadderValue` (note), `factRefiled`, `introductionDropped`, `milestoneGroup`, `milestoneIdMapped`, `milestoneDropped`, `milestoneAmbiguousId` (note), `milestoneFromPlan`, `milestoneFromPlanSkipped` (note), `optionType`. One stat change can carry two repairs (a seat form filed under `shared` counts `statIdSeatForm` and `statGroupMoved`); a dropped change counts only its drop.
- **Notes, not repairs:** an outcome id held in two lists (the template's problem, not the reply's) and a planned milestone that could not be used (nothing in the reply changed). Unit 5's `noRepairs` ignores notes, as the plan says for off-ladder values.
- **Unknown milestone ids are mapped only on turns that may add milestones** (the ending, a switch after the first beat). On any other turn there is no just-ended thread, so an unknown id is dropped.
- **Several ended threads on one outcome** (parallel threads pushing one shared outcome): the reply's milestones on that outcome are taken to cover those threads in plan order, and the rest get their planned milestones.
- **Seat forms:** a seat form naming a seat that is not in the story (`player3_energy` in a two-player story) is dropped as unknown; in single player a player stat under any seat goes to player1 (`statGroupMoved`).
- **Introductions of player slots** (`player2` introduced to player1) are dropped with the other unknown ids, as the plan's rule reads: slots are not story elements. The prompt's "introduced to" list then no longer shows them.
- **TR-10 log line:** `[PlayerManager] Filled <n> background stat value(s) that <slot>'s background left out` (count and seat, no stat ids).
- **Shared code:** `isPlayerBeat` and `canAddMilestones` are exported from `storyTextSteps.ts`, so the schema and the repair read one rule each. `expectedOptionType` in `beatRepairs.ts` is a copy of the eval's until unit 5 imports it. Test fixtures `stat()` and `outcome()` are in `server/tests/helpers/textFixtures.ts` for the later units.
- **Dry run:** after unit 1, `npm run eval:text` (no `--run`) builds every stage; stages 0-3 have no pending jobs (all stored), Stage 4 plans its 95 jobs. No anchored passage changed in this unit.

Unit 2 (what the state shows the models, ST-1 to ST-8). Decisions beyond the plan:

- **Empty switch and thread instructions** print no line either (ST-4's "an empty list prints no line" applied to the same section): today an empty `switchAndThreadInstructions` list, which every mock story and any template without such instructions carries, printed a stray `- ` line under "SPECIAL SWITCH/THREAD INSTRUCTIONS:". The section's empty entries are filtered, so an empty thread-type list leaves no blank line. The instructions keep their one `- item` per line form.
- **Guidelines:** a missing World line no longer leaves a blank line in STORY GUIDELINES (the section now filters its empty entries so that an empty list prints no line).
- **Possible values** sit right after the stat's header line in the detailed view (before "Narrative"), trimmed; a value of only whitespace counts as blank.
- **Thread type line** prints only when the resolved thread has a non-empty `typeOfThread`.
- **Dry run:** no anchored passage changed; `npm run eval:text` (no `--run`) builds every stage, stages 0-3 have no pending jobs, Stage 4 plans its 95 jobs.

Unit 3 (stories start with the outcomes they need, SS-1 to SS-3). Decisions beyond the plan:

- **Helpers for units 4 and 5** (`core/utils/outcomeReadiness.ts`): the problems are the exported constants `NO_OUTCOMES_PROBLEM` and `NO_SHARED_OUTCOME_PROBLEM`, lower-case clauses written to follow a colon, so unit 4's "Your previous plan could not be used: <problem>." reads the same way. `templateStartProblem(template, playerCount)` takes any object with `sharedOutcomes` and `player<N>.outcomes`, so EV-4 can pass the setup reply as it is. `storyStateStartProblem(state, playerCount)` (not in the plan) reads a created state's `players[slot].outcomes`; `generateStoryState` uses it. No contested-outcome helper yet: unit 4 adds one there if PL-10 needs it.
- **`withOneRetry(attempt, problemOf, what)`** takes a third argument, the thing being generated ("story setup"; unit 4: "switch plan" / "thread plan"), for the error `Failed to generate a usable <what>`. The error is an `UnusableResultError` with the second problem on `.problem`, never in the message, because plan problems will name model-written ids. `attempt` receives the first problem as its argument on the second call (the setup ignores it: a fresh sample, no prompt change). A call that throws is not retried there (the chat model already retries failed requests). The caller logs each problem inside its `problemOf` (the setup logs `Setup for story <id> can't start: <problem>`; start problems carry no ids).
- **The 400** reads `This World can't start a story for <n> player(s) yet: <problem>.`; the client's API error handler shows it as an error notification. As with today's player-count 400, the route still counts the request against the `initialize_story` rate limit (pre-existing, not changed).
- **Editor issues come from the rule per player count**, not a separate copy of it: `templateStartProblem` runs for each count from `playerCountMin` to `playerCountMax`, and each problem becomes one issue naming the stories it stops ("Stories", "Single-player stories", "Multiplayer stories", or "Stories for 1 or 2 players"). The issue is an error when no allowed count can start and a warning when some can. This gives the plan's three cases exactly (no outcomes anywhere: error; multiplayer-only without a shared outcome: error; single player allowed: warning) and also covers seats with uneven outcomes (a template whose only outcome is on player3 errors for 1 and 2 players).
- **Duplicate outcome ids**: one warning listing each id and its lists (`the_crown (shared, player1)`), over the shared list and the seats up to `playerCountMax`; an id repeated inside one list is not flagged (the plan names only ids across lists). Blank ids are skipped.
- **`.context/api.md`**: the template endpoint gained its error cases (400 player count or start rule, 404, 500).
- **Visual check of the validation card not done**: the Playwright MCP server failed to connect in this session. The card renders the new issues through its existing error and warning lists (category `outcomes` is not clickable, like every category but images). Worth a look at desktop and mobile widths on a template with no outcomes.
- **Dry run:** no anchored passage changed; `npm run eval:text` (no `--run`) builds every stage, stages 0-3 have no pending jobs, Stage 4 plans its 95 jobs.

Unit 4 (plans are checked, repaired or retried once, PL-1 to PL-13). Decisions beyond the plan:

- **Repair kinds** (`server/src/game/services/planChecks.ts`), which unit 5 counts as `repair:<kind>`. Switches: `switchSlotUnknown`, `switchPlayerRepeated`, `switchDropped`, `flavorDirectionsCleared`, `directionJunk`, `directionUnknownOutcomes`, `directionsTrimmed`; notes `directionManyOutcomes`, `directionNoOutcome`. Threads: `threadSlotUnknown`, `threadPlayerRepeated`, `threadDropped`, `threadIdDuplicate`, `durationFromSteps`, `contestSidesSwapped`. Both: note `outcomeChecksSkipped`.
- **Where the retry lives:** `checkedSwitchPlan` / `checkedThreadPlan` in `planChecks.ts` wrap the call, the check, the logging and `withOneRetry`, so `AIStoryGenerator` passes one invoke function each and the protocol is unit-tested with a fake call. The retry line goes after the whole prompt (after the story state), as the plan's "appended to the prompt" says; nothing anchors on it.
- **One problem line:** every problem the check finds is joined with "; " into the one retry line, so the planner can fix all of them in its second reply. Problems are lower-case clauses naming the switch or thread by id and, for an unknown outcome, the story's outcome ids ("… names the outcome id "x", which is not in this story; use one of: a, b, c"). The first-thread problem in a competitive story lists only the contested shared outcomes.
- **Players:** slots the story doesn't have are dropped from switches as from threads (the plan names it for threads only), and a switch or thread left without players is dropped. A player on both sides of one thread stays on side A (side A is read first). A single player's extra switches and threads fall to the same first-place rule, so they count as `…PlayerRepeated` plus `…Dropped`.
- **Junk directions:** field names match case-sensitively (they are JSON keys), so "Title fight at the harbor …" and "Identify the forger …" stay. The 12-letter count includes the letters of bracketed ids. A non-string entry counts as junk (for raw eval replies).
- **Ids in directions** are read as the rating page reads them: the story's outcome ids anywhere in the direction as whole words, plus id-like words in brackets. A direction is dropped only when it has bracketed id-like words and names no known outcome; one known id beside an unknown one is kept without a note.
- **Result kinds:** a results object belongs to the one kind whose three keys it holds; anything else (no kind, or two) is a mixed-kind problem. The contest sides are swapped only when the contest is otherwise usable (a contest mode, side A filled, contest results throughout).
- **The first-thread rule's predicate** (`!story.hasThreadAnalysis()`) also holds at turn 0, so the eval's synthetic turn-0 thread case keeps its branch; the Stage 3 trim T1 applies only once the story has a thread plan. `isContestedOutcome(outcome)` was added to `core/utils/outcomeReadiness.ts` for PL-10.
- **Log lines:** repairs as `[LLM] repair {…}` (counts only); the skip note as `[LLM] plan outcome checks skipped: story <id> holds no outcomes`, once per story per process (a set of story ids capped at 5,000, oldest dropped first); a plan that can't be used as `[Story] The <switch|thread> plan for story <id> at turn <n> could not be used`, without the problem.
- **Dry run:** `npm run eval:text` (no `--run`) builds every stage's rows, stages 0-3 have no pending jobs, Stage 4 plans its 95 jobs. Multiplayer first-thread prompts (turn 1) now carry the first-thread rule, so they differ from what the stored `postfix` records measured (unit 5's EV-6 retires that tag for `--run`).

Unit 5 (the eval reads what the game keeps, EV-1 to EV-6). Decisions beyond the plan:

- **Count names:** repairs count as `repair:<kind>` and notes as `note:<kind>` (not `repair:<kind>` as the unit 1 note above expected), so a note such as `offLadderValue` or `outcomeChecksSkipped` never reads as a repair of the reply. `noRepairs` ignores notes; `planUsable` is added only for switch and thread replies (`withRepairs(result, repairs, plan?)` in `textChecks.ts`).
- **Zero fill:** the report averages a count over the replies that carry it (`meanCountsOf` in `armStats.ts`), so `checksForRecords` counts every kind a role's replies show on each of that role's replies, 0 where absent. Without it a kind's mean would read per repaired reply, and the variant section would drop a kind the reference never showed.
- **One chain rule:** `storyAfterAnalysis(story, kind, analysis)` in `jobPlan.ts` is the one place a chain applies an analysis; the chain's beat build and `outputChecks`' pipeline beat input both call it. A plan with a problem is applied as written (the raw reply, not its partly repaired form), as the plan's "as is" reads.
- **Not changed:** the case builder (`caseBuilder.ts`) still applies the baseline's raw switch and thread replies when it builds template cases. The cases are frozen, and a `--rebuild-cases` would change every input anyway; worth moving onto `storyAfterAnalysis` if cases are ever rebuilt. The rating pages still show raw replies (out of scope in the plan).
- **Dry run rows** stayed on the `postfix` tag, which `--run` now refuses. The review fix moved them to `--prompt-state` (default `round0`; see Controversial Decisions). The dry-run total line reads `of $33 (hard cap, raised from $30 by the owner on 2026-09-27; the first target was about $25)`.
- **Retired tags** live in `variants.ts` (`retiredPromptStateProblem`), so the refusal is unit-tested; `run.ts` only calls it. Both messages suggest `round0`.
- **Re-check of the stored outputs** (free, a temporary Jest file, deleted): all 2,660 usable stored outputs pass through the new checks and all 669 stored analysis replies through `storyAfterAnalysis` without throwing, including the slim, minimal and rewrite shapes. Repair rates are in `.context/text-model-eval.md`, "Checks on what the game keeps". No stored plan is unusable under the new checks.
- **Dry run:** `npm run eval:text` (no `--run`) builds every stage's rows, stages 0-3 have no pending jobs, Stage 4 plans its 95 jobs, total `$26.38 of $33`.

Round 0 play fixes: the two noted-only items (a failed setup left the client on "queued"; background values unchecked by type). Decisions beyond the owner's brief:

- **Where a failure is kept:** in the server process (`server/src/stories/setupStatus.ts`), like game sessions and the story cache; the story's DB entries are still deleted on failure, as before. Failures are kept for the latest 5,000 story ids. A status poll for an id that is neither stored nor being set up in this process now reads `failed` / `setup_lost` instead of `queued`, which also ends the wait after a server restart mid-setup (the same stuck screen, from another cause).
- **Failure log:** one line, `Setup of story <id> failed for good`, with no error object. The old line printed the whole error, which can quote the premise or the model's reply, so debugging detail is gone from that line; the chat model's own call logs are unchanged. The request's pre-existing start line (`Creating new story with prompt: "<first 50 characters>..."`) still quotes the premise; it is not new, so I left it. Worth removing if the owner wants no premise text in logs at all. (Removed on 2026-09-27 with three more; see Setup rounds under Controversial Decisions.)
- **Polling errors:** a failed status request is polled again; three in a row end the wait with the same failure screen (reason `unreachable`, client-side only). Before, one failed request left the screen on "Waiting..." for good. The price: if the server was only briefly away, Try again can start a second story while the first finishes.
- **Try again** re-sends the last request exactly (merged premise and settings, or the template request), so it passes the content filter and the `initialize_story` rate limit again and counts against it. **Back** returns to the form the story came from, still filled in (setup step 3, or the template configurator). The failed story's codes are removed from local storage, so the story list doesn't offer a dead code.
- **Other paths:** template stories are created before the response (`ready`, or an HTTP error shown on the form, unchanged), so they never waited on "queued"; their dead polling branch now shares the new poller and the failure screen. Multiplayer creation is the same path as single player for the creator; an invited player whose story failed (or isn't ready yet) gets the game page's existing invalid-code error, which doesn't hang.
- **Type rule details** (`core/utils/statValueCheck.ts`): a number or percentage outside 0 to 100 is replaced by the stat's initial value, not clamped (the brief converts only "within its range"; superseded on 2026-09-27, when the owner accepted clamping: see Setup rounds under Controversial Decisions); number stats have no range. A number for a string stat is not converted (3 could mean a ladder step); a list with any non-string item is replaced whole. Opposites `"a|b"` converts only when a + b = 100 (stored as a, the stat's convention). "Unknown" means not a player stat id, so a shared stat's value in a background is dropped too. A value a background gives for a universal player stat (`partOfPlayerBackgrounds: false`) is kept and still wins over the stat's initial value, as before, but is type-checked.
- **Logs name counts, not stat ids:** "logged once (ids only)" is read as story or template id, seat and background index, with counts per kind (`player1 background 0: 1 converted, 1 dropped`), following round 0's rule that stat ids carry story words (the TR-10 test asserts the same). The fill of missing background stats (TR-10) now also reads the stat's initial value by type.
- **Where the check runs:** character selection, story start from a template, a generated setup before it is stored (in `StoryCreationService`, since `AIStoryGenerator.ts` belonged to the model-migration run), and `TemplateService` create, create-or-update and update (AI-generated templates and AI iterations are saved through these). ZIP imports copy files and are not checked at import; story start covers them.
- **Editor:** one `backgrounds` warning per background, not auto-fixable, saying what each value becomes and that saving corrects it. Unknown stat ids keep the existing reference errors (there were two already), and shared stats in a background the orphaned-stat warning. After a save through the update endpoint, the editor's form can still hold the old values until reload, so the warning (and, on that path, the unsaved-changes marker) can stay until the page reloads.
- **One pre-existing type error fixed:** `TemplateService.createFullTemplateObject` indexed the template by slot in a way the tests' stricter type-check rejects (TS7053). It surfaced once a test imported the service; a cast to `Record<string, unknown>` fixes it with no behaviour change.
- **No visual check of the failure screen:** the Playwright MCP server failed to connect in this session and ToolSearch found no Playwright tools. The screen's copy, buttons and `role="alert"` are pinned by static-render tests (`StorySetupFailed.test.tsx`, and the StoryInitializer and TemplateConfigurator tests). Worth a look at desktop and mobile widths.

## Round groundwork, part 1 (implementer, 2026-09-27)

The shared, free groundwork for the setup and turn rounds ("Before round 1" in both improvement documents): pairwise pages, the round stages, and the deterministic new checks with their baselines. No API call; no production behaviour change (two helpers in `planChecks.ts` are now exported for the eval). Decisions beyond the coordinator's brief:

- **Pairwise pages** (`--rating-page … --pairwise`): exactly two arms, refused otherwise (a pairwise page with rotating candidates would mix references). The answer is one "Which is better?" per item with A, B or "About the same", not ranks, so a tie is a choice rather than a missing rank. The repeat shows the first item's pair with its labels swapped (the ranked page's hash position could land on the same order, which tests memory rather than the rater's reading). The default is nine setup items and 14 turn items, from the two documents' round formats. The owner's criteria are fixed text in `PAIRWISE_CRITERIA` and pass the blinding word check.
- **`--no-repeat`** works on every page (ranked too); the repeat stays on by default, as setup doc S3 recommended.
- **The pairwise scorer** reports a sign test (two-sided, exact, ties left out) beside the win, tie and loss counts, because nine items make a 6-3 split look stronger than it is (p = 0.51). Ranked pages score as before; `--score` picks the scorer from the key's mode.
- **The migration stage:** the migration agent added only the `filter` ledger stage (for the content-filter check, with no `--run`), so there was no stage for checking the migrated text roles. `migration` runs production's code defaults (`TEXT_MODEL_GROUPS`, not env) on the `prod` form at two samples, with single-player chains; its cap is $1.20 against a dry-run estimate of $0.75 plus AI Iteration and setup's low estimates. Defined now because the round pages need references in today's form (below); nothing runs until the coordinator starts it.
- **Stored references:** `--check-baselines` compares the stored `postfix` production-form request hashes with what today's code builds. Setup: 18 of 18 identical, so the setup rounds can read the stored Luna low and Sol low setups as their references at no cost. Beats, switches and threads: 0 identical (the round-0 state text changed every turn and plan prompt), so the turn rounds need the migration check's `round0` records as references, or must accept that difference on the page. **Coordinator's call.**
- **No baseline in the new stages** (`stageRunsBaseline`): `--run` in Stages 0-4 plans gpt-4.1 / gpt-4.1-mini on every case, which in a new stage would have spent about $2.9 of the $3 setup cap on gpt-4.1 setups the owner no longer wants run. The runner's "candidates only where the baseline worked" gate is off in these stages too, so a new built case (the turn doc's late switch, exploration and trigger cases) can run without a baseline record.
- **The round stages plan nothing yet:** their candidates come with the eval-only variants they test (part 2). Their caps and reasons are in `STAGE_CAP_REASONS`, and the dry run prints each reason beside the spend.
- **Check definitions the documents left open** (all in `setupDesignChecks.ts` and `turnDesignChecks.ts`, each with a test):
  - A three-player race counts as a contest when a shared three-path outcome asks Who or Which (setup doc A1.3's interim form).
  - `scoreStatPerContest` wants at least one scoreboard in a two-player contest mode even when the slate has no contested outcome. Otherwise such a setup passes vacuously, which read Luna at 88% against the setup doc's 0 of 3.
  - `noMirroredOutcomes` and the per-seat counter test take out every identity name, premise-named players included (the Casablanca mirrors), while `noSlotNames` and `noIdentityNamesInOutcomes` let premise names pass (S8). Name words match as written and skip ordinary words (Will, May, Hope and others), so "Will …?" questions don't read as the identity "Will Carter".
  - `sacrificeNoBonus` counts "chance", "potentially", "possibly" and a signed number as well as "bonus", "risk" and "might". It reads higher than the document's 54 of 80 gpt-4.1 stats (about 80%). (Narrowed on 2026-09-27, below: "points", "risky" and "may" no longer count.)
  - `triggerStatsExist` reads only numeric thresholds on a capitalised name ("When Public Support falls below 30"), so an NPC's state ("If Gruk is hurt") is not mistaken for a stat.
  - `noExampleCopy` reads the example block of the prompt each setup was sent (`prompts/<hash>.txt`), from "EXAMPLE STAT SETUPS" or "WORKED EXAMPLE" to "Character Selection Instructions", so a candidate's worked example is checked against itself.
  - `milestoneNotCopied` reads the reply as written (the one exception to C7), since a milestone the repair adds from the plan is a copy by construction.
  - `sacrificeApplied` wants a change on the stat the chosen option's text names, or any known stat when it names none; after the round-0 repairs it passes everywhere, and the raw losses read as `repair:statIdSeatForm`.
  - Binding late (`lateDirectionsOnNeeded`) uses the turn doc's arithmetic (chapters that fit = turns left ÷ 4; still needed = intended − recorded − the pending one). It applies to 22 of 36 stored switch cases, because today's setups plan more milestones than chapters fit. So it is not a late-story reading until the setup rounds scale the slate to the length.
- **Not built** (part 2, or new cases or fields first): the judged checks and their calibration; the chapter question and plan fields; the ending's per-outcome answers; triggers fired on built trigger cases; `challengeShare` in the setup play chain; trait repetition and callbacks (the B5 counts that need the story's history). The rules the game already repairs read as the repair counts.
- **Stat readouts:** the sample in `check-baselines.md` holds every hit on the stored isolated beats (31), and all read as real readouts, so no false-alarm rate could be measured on this data beyond "rare".
- **The dry run** builds every stage's rows; the round rows read 0 jobs, and the migration rows read 214 isolated jobs (est $0.65) and 21 chains (est $0.10), plus 10 AI Iteration jobs (est $0.02) under `--role iteration`.

## Round groundwork, part 2 (implementer, 2026-09-27)

The turn rounds' preparation, turn doc section 4 "Before round 1" items 4 to 7: the missing cases, the chapter backfill, the judged checks' calibration and the timing logs. Spent $0.10 in `turn-rounds` (build $0.024, backfill $0.011, calibration $0.068); the ledger reads $26.49 of $33. No production behaviour change. Decisions beyond the coordinator's brief:

- **Where the new calls go:** the build's few calls are production-form story calls and go to `calls.jsonl` like the original build's; the backfill and the judge go to a new `prep-calls.jsonl` (runner group `prep`, arm keys `backfill>…`, `judge>…`), because every report reads `calls.jsonl` by arm and role and would have counted them as thread plans and beats. Their spend is added wherever the caps are checked, in the dry run and in `results.md`.
- **Round cases stay out of Stages 0 to 4** (`casesFor`): otherwise the closed stages' dry-run rows would grow by baseline and Luna jobs on the new cases. The migration check and the rounds plan them; the migration rows grow by 30 isolated jobs and 11 chains (about $0.10 more), five of the jobs done by the build's own calls, which are recorded under the round case's id with production's default arm at sample 1 (the first-beat convention).
- **Missing history is written by production's GPT-6 defaults** (`productionArm`), not the gpt-4.1-mini baseline the original build used (owner: no gpt-4.x in new runs), and only where no stored reply exists: the Neonate chapter plan and the three-player story's second and third chapter steps, switch plan, switch turn and chapter plan (6 calls, one a network error retried). The first three-player chapter was played to its end rather than cut to its one stored step, so the switch after it reads a real 3-step chapter. A finished build call is reused only on the same prompt (the build's dice set every later state); otherwise the build stops.
- **Edited cases' choices:** bases are Novi Reg (story 8988006e; the switch at turn 8 after its second chapter, the switch at turn 4 after a favorable first chapter, the chapter plan at turn 5) and IPO to Mars (no switch/thread instructions of its own). Late switches as "turns left" (5 and 3), not absolute lengths. The short and 20-turn stories take the setup doc's A10 budget, `milestoneBudget` (3 as 1/1/1, 5 as 2/2/1), not the turn doc's "10 turns → 2 chapters", since each of three outcomes needs one; at 10 turns the stored flavor switch's outcome is then already complete, so that chapter plan also tests the aftermath milestone. The new-form rules are my wording of setup doc A7.1/A7.3 for these stories (in `roundCases.ts`); each trigger case changes one thing against the same base.
- **The exploration step** is step 2 of the one stored exploration chapter (Café de Paris), whose steps and milestones are exploration while its outcome is favorable/mixed/unfavorable: a mixed-kind chapter (A1's `oneKindPerChapter` fails on it). No purely exploration chapter exists to build from.
- **Chapter frames** are attached when the cases are read (`evalFiles.readCases`), not written into the frozen case files, so the frozen cases stay byte for byte and the frames can be regenerated. 14 of 19 chapters had the planner's own input; the first chapters of the four stored stories and one synthetic branch are cut back from a state one step later (stats, facts and elements as of that state). One frame ends in a stray "R"; kept as the model wrote it.
- **Judged checks:** one call per player's turn covering the checks that apply, each phrased so yes passes, evidence quoted before the answer; the prompt version is part of the call's key. The calibration set is the notes' 28 hand-read turns; gpt-4.1-mini's progress verdicts (the notes give only a count) are my reading of its seven chapter steps (S3 and S6 yes, S5 no, the rest ambiguous), marked as such in the set. The first reliability bar (85% agreement with the hand on sample 1, 90% between two samples) was set before the first run; it is now held per side, yes and no (verification fixes, below).
  - v1 (Luna low): no check passed. The first-paragraph misses were choices of direction (switch options, no roll), which the hand read counts as narrated when the character takes the direction up; M3's gpt-4.1-mini "settles" turned out to be the next step played early, not this step settled, and now reads ambiguous. v2 fixed both: the first-paragraph check became reliable on Luna low (21 of 22, samples 24 of 25).
  - `stepLeftOpen` was not reliable on Luna low or Luna medium at v2 (65% and 76%) or at v3, which asks whether the options still decide how the step is carried out (76% on both). Luna low answered with no reasoning on 39 of 56 v1 calls. The misses are one boundary: a first move against the step carried out. I stopped there: a fourth wording on 17 items would fit the calibration set rather than the check.
  - `concreteProgress` agrees on 7 to 9 of 10 but flips between samples on two Luna turns where a conversation opened and no ally was won yet, and has one hand no, so a false pass is untested.
  - **Coordinator's call:** round 1 judges the first paragraph on Luna low (v3). For the other two: report them as readings beside the calibration numbers, not as gates, and let the owner's rating ("Does this turn move the chapter's question, and follow its plan?") carry them; or have the owner mark about ten step turns on round 1's page for both questions, which would give the negatives the calibration lacks, and re-calibrate then (about $0.02).
- **Timing logs (item 7):** none locally (no log file; `data/stories` and `data/templates` are empty here), and `[TurnTiming]` exists only on this branch, so production has never written it. The owner can count production's older per-choice lines on main in the host's logs ("Found complete pregenerated state for player1, using it" against "No complete pregenerated state found for player1, choice <n>"); multiplayer prints the miss line for every seat, so the hit share read that way is a lower bound. Until then, section 3.3's interlude question stays open.

## Verification fixes (implementer, 2026-09-27)

Five verified findings on the migration and the round groundwork. No API call (the two reports below were re-rendered from stored records; `--judge-calibration` ran with nothing open and `--max-spend 0`); the ledger still reads $26.49 of $33. Decisions beyond the findings:

- **Render variables, when:** delete before or at the merge, never set a GPT-6 value while main is live. Deleting early is safe (main falls back to its gpt-4.1 defaults); a GPT-6 value on main fails every call (temperature always sent, no effort, GPT-6's default effort rejects temperature) and the fail-closed filter then refuses every story. Fixed in `.context/ai-transparency.md` §11, `.context/deployment.md` and the settings note (`DOCS/2026-09-27_gpt6-migration-settings.md`, local).
- **Cut-off replies in the logs:** the retry line and a failed call's `error` now carry `finishReason` ("length", or "content_filter") and the error's class as `name` when its name is the generic "Error" (openai's errors set none; a 500 now reads `InternalServerError`). I logged the class, not the message: some messages quote the model's reply, and the call log carries no text. The settings note's "watch for" list now points at the retry lines, since the successful attempt's `metrics.finishReason` can never read "length".
- **The judge's reliability bar** is now held per side: 85% of the hand-yes items and 85% of the hand-no items, at least 3 on each side, plus 90% self-agreement. 85% overall sat below the majority-class baseline (an always-yes judge cleared it on the first-paragraph and progress checks). The minimum of 3 is my choice: it is the first-paragraph check's count, so that check can still pass, and `concreteProgress` (1 hand no) now reads "too few hand no" instead of a plain "not reliable". No reading of the stored runs changed. The report now lists the hand-no items per check: every one is a gpt-4.1-mini turn, so the first-paragraph check's false-pass rate on Luna is untested; the Coordinator's call above (the owner marking round 1 step turns) is also the way to add Luna negatives for that check.
- **`sacrificeNoBonus`** no longer counts "points", "risky" or "may" (setup doc A5's own worked example failed it). Permissive "may" and uncertain "may" can't be told apart by a word list, so a hedged "may be lost" now passes; "might", "chance", "potentially", "possibly", "risk" and a signed number still fail. Re-run over 2,664 stored replies: gpt-4.1 / Luna low / Luna medium / Sol low 0% / 22% / 44% / 86% (was 0% / 14% / 28% / 86%); against the rank-1 picks 3 agree, 4 against (was 2, 5).
- **`--judge-calibration --max-spend`** now refuses on every requested judge's estimate together before sending anything, and each judge runs on what the earlier ones left (`capsAfter`, shared with `--build-round-cases`).

## Adoption into production (implementer, 2026-09-28)

The carried-forward forms move from the eval into production, one commit per step, test-first. No API call and no spend in this workflow (ledger $31.99 of $33); B10, the paid final check and the Casablanca retest did not run.

- **Step 0, the eval keeps what it measured: today's form frozen as `storyTextRound0/`.** Every eval variant edits production's text at anchors, so adopting the forms into production's prompt services would have broken or silently changed them. Production's four prompt services were copied word for word into `server/src/game/services/storyTextRound0/` (`Round0SetupPromptService`, `Round0SwitchPromptService`, `Round0ThreadPromptService`, `Round0BeatPromptService`) with the request builders in `round0Steps.ts`; the schemas stay core's zod instances, which the adoption leaves unchanged (production extends them server-side), and the story state stays production's renderer, whose default sections the adoption leaves unchanged. The eval's `prod` variant, the setup and turn rounds, the Stage 3 trims, the Stage 4 rewrite, the chapter backfill, the case builders and the probe's iteration schema now build from the frozen copy; `apply`, the plan checks and the beat repairs stay production's.
  - **Why `prod` stays the frozen form rather than following production:** every stored `prod` record (the rounds' references, today's form in every report) must keep rebuilding byte for byte, since `rebuiltToday`, the stored references and the chain's group turns read it; and every variant name keeps its meaning. `round0` is therefore not retired: every variant under it still builds what it built. A new variant `adopted` builds what production sends now, read against `prod`, for the paid final check; no stage plans it, and its records need a new prompt state.
  - **Checked:** a temporary snapshot (deleted) hashed every variant's prompt and JSON schema on the 129 frozen cases, the four chain premises and a matrix of setup and iteration inputs (3,155 requests): 0 changed after the freeze, and `adopted` equal to `prod` before any adoption. The dry run plans no open job in the rounds and the prep (backfill 0 open), as before.
- **Step 1a, the template editor shows and edits contested outcomes** (setup doc B1.11; status note section 9: before templates use the new form). A contested outcome showed empty challenge fields and its edits were dropped. The kinds and their fields now live in one helper (`client/src/resources/templates/utils/outcomeResolutions.ts`): challenge, contest ("Side A wins" / "Mixed" / "Side B wins", side A being player1's side, or player1's camp with three players) and exploration. Shared outcomes offer all three kinds; a player's own outcome offers challenge and exploration, and keeps "Contest" selectable only when it already holds one (the game's contests are shared outcomes). Switching kind starts with that kind's empty fields, as before. The resolution tooltip names the contest kind. **No browser check:** the Playwright server failed to connect and ToolSearch finds no browser tool; the static render test covers the first paint only.
- **Step 1b, the final setup form in production** (custom stories, templates, AI Iteration): setup round 3 as measured (`setupR3`, round 2b's arm B on `ROUND3_PARTS`), written as plain text in `prompts/setupPromptText.ts` and `StorySetupPromptService.ts` and as field descriptions over core's zod instances in `setupSchema.ts` (core's schemas and descriptions stay as they are: they define the stored shapes and the frozen round0 form reads them). `adoptedSetup.test.ts` holds production byte for byte equal to the measured requests, prompt and JSON schema, over every player count and mode, 11 story lengths, with and without the kids budget, for stories and templates, and over all 127 section sets for AI Iteration; the assembly equals the eval's.
  - **What production does differently:** the round-3 form as the round-3 report lists it (engine facts, outcome slates sized by the story length, one opposites scoreboard per contest with two camps for three players, stats that act, the one-line catalogue and the worked example in place of the two example setups, stake questions, steering fields, the generation order, the no-blank-items line, the identity clause, conflict rules and hook facts with NPC pronouns in the role, no "energy" in the examples); the story length reaches the prompt (it was dropped before); a read-with-kids custom story (its category) gets the kids stat budget (`createInitialState` takes `{ kids }`); templates have no category at generation and AI Iteration never gets the budget, as measured.
  - **The reply is put back into today's fields before anything reads it** (`setupStep.request(...).assemble`, applied by `AIStoryGenerator` for stories and templates, and to the development mock file): one deliberate difference from the eval's assembly, an empty `sharedOutcomes` list where a single player's reply has none (setup doc B1.8: the template and story code read the list), so `TemplateService.generateTemplate` and the story state get `[]`.
  - **"The seat roles saved where the game keeps its selection plan"** read as the eval's assembly does: the roles go into `characterSelectionPlan.multiplayerCoordination`. The game drops that plan after generation, as it always did (stories and templates), so the roles live on in each background's text and the scoreboard's tooltip, which the form asks to name them; keeping the plan would be a new stored field. Not built.
  - **AI Iteration's request** is now `iterationStep.request` (prompt and cut schema together); `partialTemplateSchema` takes the game mode, and `generatePartialTemplateUpdate` takes the request.
  - **Not adopted (untested, the owner's call):** the Casablanca clause ("use those names in outcomes and stats" → "in outcomes"; the retest is the Casablanca premise ×2, about $0.011, which the setup-rounds stage can pay but this workflow's brief did not), and varying or dropping the kids examples ("Courage" named a stat in 4 of 4 kids setups). Production ships the measured form, with its known Casablanca regression (per-player named stats when the premise names the players), until the owner says yes to the retest.
  - **The two setups that still repeat one name** (a shed setup "Rowan" ×3, a cancellation setup "Alex Moreno" ×3) read: neither premise names the character or uses a role word the clause could miss. The cancellation premise is written in the first person ("I'm trying to cancel…"), which may read as one person across identities; the shed premise names no one. Nothing to change without a measured fix.
- **Step 2, templates and AI Iteration on GPT-6 Sol low:** already production's default since 807e44a (`GENERATION_MODEL_*` = gpt-6-sol@low in `textModelSettings.ts`, with its tests, `.context/ai-transparency.md` row 5, `.context/deployment.md` and the settings note in `DOCS/`); nothing to change in code. **The limits re-checked against the new form:** the only Sol low setups on an adopted-form ancestor are round 1's nine (`setupR1`): slowest 84.0 s, longest 7,173 output tokens (median 6,178). On Luna low, round 3's form wrote 6% more at the median than round 1's (7,066 against 6,669 tokens) and no more at the longest (9,650 against 9,620), so Sol on round 3's form should stay near 7,600 tokens and about 90 s, inside the editor's 150 s timeout (1.8× round 1's slowest Sol setup) and 20,000-token cap (2.8× its longest). No Sol setup on round 3's form has run (no money), so the first AI Drafts after deploy are the first reading: watch `[LLM]` lines with role templateGeneration for `ms` and output tokens.
- **Step 3, planner v2 in production** (the switch and chapter planners, single and multiplayer): planner v2 with two-sided contests as the setup-round-3 chain ran it (`planV2b`), written as plain text in `prompts/SwitchPromptService.ts` and `prompts/ThreadPromptService.ts`, the replies in `plannerReplies.ts`, the arithmetic in `pacing.ts` (a production copy of the eval's `storyTextRounds/pacing.ts`, with its tests copied too, so the measured copy stays as it ran). `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` holds production byte for byte equal to the measured requests on 19 built stories and every frozen switch (27) and chapter (19) case, with the one logged delta below, and the assembly equal to the eval's.
  - **What production does differently:** directions carry the one outcome they push, from the story's outcome ids as a list, and the switch turn reads them as today's "text (id)" strings; code sets a single player's chapter outcome from the pick (a group's plan names it, as a list, and the plan check holds it); both planners read the computed PACING block (binding late priority, the chapter lengths PACING allows, the last chapter taking the turns left, milestone sizes); continuity is stated once in its narrow form; a chapter is one situation rising to a climax, its kind follows its outcome's, and a contest has two sides (with three players the setup's two camps); lean replies (no per-player notes, restated lists or restated instructions; the last step a title and a question whose results are the milestones), assembled into today's plan shape with kind, question and plan riding along; the planners' view drops the image library, names the ended chapter "THE THREAD THAT JUST ENDED" with each beat's chosen option only (new opt-in `SectionConfig` flags `switchPacing` and `threadJustEndedForSwitches`; `getSwitchConfiguration` takes the chapter planner's PLAYER DECISIONS block), and the multiplayer first-thread reminder that repeated the MANDATORY block goes.
  - **The two logged adoption items:** (1) the plan check with one retry covers the chapter-length rule (`checkThreadPlan(..., { lengths: true })`, production only): a disallowed length is told to the one retry, but a length alone never fails the turn (the second reply is used if its only problem is its length, noted `lengthNotAllowed`; the first is used if its only problem was its length and the second can't be used). The eval's readings (`planUsable`, the chains' `storyAfterAnalysis`) call the check without the option, so they read as measured; `lengthAllowed` stays the eval's own design check. (2) "without a number" in the chapter title's field ("The thread's title, without a number: its beats show it with their number."), the one byte difference to `planV2b`, pinned in the test.
  - **`outcomeIdsNamed` moved** to `outcomeIds.ts` (planChecks re-exports it), so `planChecks` and `pacing` import each other's module without a cycle.
- **Step 4, the option rules (B6) in the beat prompt**, as the chain ran them (`turnB6`, today's turn form with B6 alone): single-player challenge and contest chapter steps only (`optionRules.ts`, `takesOptionRules`); every other single-player turn and every group turn stays today's form. `adoptedTurns.test.ts` holds production equal to `turnB6` on nine built single-player stories and all 46 frozen single-player turns, and to the frozen round0 form on eight built group stories and all 14 frozen group turns.
  - **What production does differently on those turns:** the prompt adds the three ways to act (after "Be specific"), the reward exception, the computed lever line (`sacrificeRewardLine`) and "don't sacrifice a stat twice in a thread", and drops today's base-point line and the licence to award bonuses the stat doesn't name; the fields carry the base-point scale, at most two bonuses from the option's approach, the reward exception in the options field, and the plan field without "(Many beats are better…)".
  - **The logged retry for a turn that comes back as one short paragraph** (`beatChecks.ts`): a player's text that shows as one paragraph, split as the client splits it, gets one more call with "Your previous reply could not be used: … Write the beats again." appended; the second reply is used even if it is short too (a short turn beats a failed one, and a turn failing after two model retries already exists for transport errors). The warning names the story and turn only.
  - **Difficulty under B6's scale: looked at, not changed.** The balance simulation (today's 129 stored challenge sets, scored with the game's own code) reads the favorable chance 44.7% → 37.4% if every tempting option took the scale's -10; in the round's own B6 options the odds stayed at today's level (44%), because the model gives a tempting option a small negative base and keeps its bonus (the one B6-alone chain: 0 / -5 / +30 at the opening, 0 / ±5 with +10 bonuses on the steps). Moving every story's difficulty on a simulated drop the measured options don't show would make the game easier than today, so the levels stay; the paid final check (44 stored turns) is the next reading of the favorable share.
- **Step 5, the scoreboard ending rule** (decision 3, question 1, settled; without B8's full ending format): the ending of a story that holds a contested shared outcome gets one line after "For shared outcomes, touch on…": "For a contested outcome, the side ahead on its scoreboard wins unless its milestones clearly say otherwise; a score between 45 and 55 is a draw (the mixed resolution). The scoreboard is the shared opposites stat its resonance names ("Scored by …"), and its first side is side A (with three players, player1's camp)." The first sentence is the setup document's A8 wording; the second tells the ending which stat and side (the setup's form names the scoreboard in the resonance and puts player1's side first). Two players and three-player camps alike (no race wording anywhere, the owner's call). Unmeasured, as decided; `adoptedTurns.test.ts` pins it as the one line production adds to today's group ending.
  - **The three setup sentences logged to change with it**, in contest setups only (competitive and cooperative-competitive multiplayer, where a scoreboard exists; single-player and cooperative setups keep the measured text): the engine block's "At the end … from the outcomes' milestones" adds "and, for a contested outcome, from its scoreboard: the side ahead wins unless the milestones clearly say otherwise"; the Outcomes section's "they are the story's only progress bar" becomes "they are the story's progress bar, and a contested outcome's scoreboard decides it at the ending unless its milestones clearly say otherwise"; the stats rule's "nothing in the game reads a stat to decide an outcome or to end the story" becomes "no stat decides an outcome or ends the story, apart from a contested outcome's scoreboard at the ending". `adoptedSetup.test.ts` holds production equal to the measured form with exactly these three swaps.
  - **With it, the scoreboard repair setup round 3's chain asked for** (`beatRepairs.ts`, `statNumberAsText`): a `setString` whose text reads as one number on a number, percentage or opposites stat becomes `setNumber` ("35|65" → 35), read the way background values are, so a won contest moves the score the ending rule reads. The repair rather than a reply-format line, as the report recommended: it also catches the older turn forms and changes no request. The eval reads what the game keeps, so stored replies that did this now count the repair.
- **Step 6, the free final test** (status note, section 9, step 3's first half): `adoptedForms.test.ts` builds every frozen case (18 setup premises with their kids tags, 5 iteration cases, 27 switch, 19 thread and 60 beat cases, and the chain's four premises) through the eval's `adopted` variant, production's own code, and holds it byte for byte equal, prompt and JSON schema, to the measured variant for its role (`setupR3`; `planV2b`; `turnB6` for one player, `prod` for groups), with the logged deltas applied from one table (`tests/helpers/adoptedDeltas.ts`, which the three per-form tests read too). It is kept in the suite and skips where the output folder is absent (the cases are gitignored); the per-form tests cover the same forms on built stories everywhere. The paid half (one sample of the 44 stored single-player turns and both planners, about $0.22) did not run: the owner has not answered the spend question.

## Verification fixes on the adoption (implementer, 2026-09-28)

Ten verified findings on adoption steps 1a to 5, seven distinct once the duplicates are merged; each fixed test-first. No API call and no spend (ledger $31.99 of $33; B10 and the paid final check still wait on the owner). `npm run check:all` is clean; the dry run builds and plans no open job in the rounds, the migration stage or the prep. Production still builds the measured requests byte for byte on every built story and frozen case (adoptedForms, adoptedPlanners, adoptedTurns), with one delta condition narrowed (the ending rule, below).

- **A single player's chapter whose pick names no outcome no longer fails for good** (findings 1 and 6). The chapter reply has no outcome field, so a pick that resolved nothing stored `""`, both attempts failed the check and every replay repeated it. Two ways in: the switch planner writes two directions (the check allows two, the switch turn always offers three, and the player picks the invented third), or a switch planned before planner v2 whose chosen direction names no id. The fix is a fallback (`pacing.ts`, `fallbackOutcomeId`): the outcome that still needs the most milestones, the main outcome on a tie, then the first listed; single player only (a group's reply names its own outcome). **Where it applies, my call:** in `pickedOutcome` itself (marked `fallback: true`), so PLAYER DECISIONS and PACING name the outcome the assembly will store and the planner writes the chapter for it, rather than only in the assembly, which would have recorded milestones written for no outcome against one chosen afterwards; the assembly also falls back when there is no pick at all. The prompt changes only in the fallback case, which no built story or frozen case reaches (the byte-for-byte tests pass unchanged). The plan check notes it (`chapterOutcomeFallback`) so the log counts it. Not chosen: capping the switch turn's options at the planned directions (a turn-prompt change, unmeasured) and an outcome field in the retry's schema (a second schema for one call). The eval's measured assembly (`turnRound1Planners.ts`, planV2b) keeps the flaw as it ran; the eval never retries a planner.
- **The plan check filters `topicDirections` with `topicChoices`** (findings 2 and 7): junk, unknown-outcome and trimmed directions leave both lists at the same positions, so the pick reads the chosen option's own outcome; a flavor switch clears both; a pair list out of step with the strings (never written by the assembly) is dropped and the pick reads the strings. Only a junk drop could shift positions (trimming cuts the end; the enum keeps unknown outcomes out), and none of 183 stored v2 directions hit the filter.
- **Contest sides, camp-aware** (finding 3): the PL-11 repair now puts player1 on side A of a contest they are in and leaves a contest player1 sits out as the planner wrote it (`withPlayer1OnSideA`, was `withLowestSlotOnSideA`). The camps live only in the setup's text (roles, backgrounds, the scoreboard's tooltip; the selection plan is dropped after generation), so no check can tell a camp-B pair from a camp-A seat, and the planner is told player1's camp is side A. Identical to the old repair for every two-player contest and every contest player1 is in. Stored eval plans with a contest between player2 and player3 read one `contestSidesSwapped` fewer on a re-render.
- **The retries never cost a usable first reply** (finding 4): a chapter plan whose only problem is its length, and a turn of one short paragraph, keep the first reply when the retry's call throws (after the chat model's two re-sends); a warning names the error's class only (`errorClass` in `retryOnce.ts`), never its message. A first plan with a real problem still fails the turn when its retry's call throws, as before.
- **Signed scoreboard text is left alone** (findings 5 and 8): `statNumberAsText` skips a text starting with + or - and a number below 0 (`"-10%"` would have set health to 0, `"+5"` gold to 5); ChangeService drops them as before. "35|65", "40%" and "12" still convert. A negative absolute on a number stat (a reputation of -10 written as text) is also left alone: it can't be told from a delta, and dropping is the old, harmless behaviour.
- **Contests only where they play** (finding 9): `contestsPlayable(gameMode, players)` in `core/utils/outcomeReadiness.ts` (two or more players, competitive or cooperative-competitive). The template editor offers the Contest kind on a shared outcome only when the World plays contests (`worldPlaysContests`, by game mode and highest player count; kept selectable on an outcome that holds one); the validation card warns about a contested shared outcome in stories that can't play it (naming, say, a competitive World's single-player stories). **The ending rule's gate, my call:** it now prints only when the story plays contests, holds a contested shared outcome and keeps a shared opposites stat (`takesScoreboardEndingRule`), so a cooperative or single-player story, or a contest a template left without a scoreboard, is not told to read a side B or a stat it doesn't have; `adoptedDeltas.isScoreboardEnding` restates it for the three adoption tests, and every frozen group ending still matches. **Not changed:** the planner's kind rule and reply schema still offer contests in every multiplayer mode (measured text); a cooperative story holding a contested outcome, reachable before step 1a by switching a template's mode, can still have its chapter refused on both attempts. The editor and the warning now keep authors out of it; a server-side guard (reading contest milestones as challenge ones outside contest modes) would be a guess at meaning and is not built.
- **Docs** (finding 10): `.context/story.md`'s stale "the one-outcome rule waits for its own field" and `planChecks.ts`'s matching comment now say the notes fire on switches planned before planner v2 and on a direction whose text names another id; story.md also records the lockstep filter, the fallback, the camp-aware sides, the retries' fallback, the signed-value rule, the ending rule's gate and the editor's Contest offer. The settings note (`DOCS/`, local) lists the two new log lines to watch.
- **No browser check:** the Playwright server failed to connect and ToolSearch finds no browser tool; the editor change is covered by the static render test (first paint) and the helper and validation tests.

## The owner's feedback of 2026-09-28 (implementer)

Four fixes from the owner's notes on `turns-r1.html` and the coordinator's settled design, one commit each, test-first. No API call in this change.

- **Fix 1, the nearer chapter question (planner v2c).** The owner's example ("Advances outcome: Does the player successfully expose and dismantle the Clandestine Waste Ring? … Kind of milestone: Milestone marking progress in exposing the Waste Ring. Chapter question: Will Arielle uncover enough concrete evidence to expose and dismantle the Clandestine Waste Ring?") is a stored today's-form chapter with its backfilled frame; planner v2 writes the same kind of question (a quarter of its plans) and copies it into the kind of milestone.
  - **In production and as `planV2c`** (built from planV2b on the frozen copy, so planV2/planV2b records reproduce): a list item "The thread's question and its kind of milestone." before the milestones (nearer than the outcome's, its three milestones answer it, about the thread's own situation, never the outcome's question in other words; a contest asks which side comes out ahead), the question field reworded the same way, a new `typeOfMilestone` reply field after it (the concrete thing the answer settles), stored as the thread's kind of milestone (the question where empty). **The worked example, my call:** the prompt's own Rikkit and the noble's conspiracy ("Will Rikkit get the noble's letters out of the manor before the guards change shifts?", kind "whether the letters prove the noble's hand in the conspiracy") rather than the coordinator's Arielle and the depot manifests, since Arielle is a stored case's story and her names in the prompt would coach the cases that measure it. **The title, my call:** planV2c carries the adoption's "without a number", so production builds planV2c byte for byte and the chapter planner's delta in `adoptedDeltas.ts` goes; measuring planV2c against planV2b therefore also measures that one clause.
  - **The checks** (`turnDesignChecks.ts`, eval only; production's plan check is unchanged, since a word heuristic that triggers a retry would be an unmeasured production change): `questionNearerThanOutcome` and `milestoneKindConcrete`, as `.context/text-model-eval.md` describes, on every thread plan and on every stored chapter's backfilled frame (`chapterFrameChecks`, a new section of `check-baselines.md`).
  - **Baselines** (`--check-baselines`, 2026-09-28, 4,003 replies): stored chapters 13 of 21 questions and 6 of 21 kinds pass; planner v2 isolated 76% ±6 (38) and 90% ±11, chains 69% (58) and 90%; today's form on Luna low kinds 84% ±11; gpt-4.1-mini 20-33%. The hand read of every flag is in the context doc: mostly real restatements, one borderline flag, three misses among 13 passes.
  - **No judged check:** the judge's calibration holds turns only, and a chapter-question judge would need hand verdicts on both sides (at least three each) before it could be called reliable. The 21 stored chapters and planner v2's flagged plans listed in the context doc are a start for such a set, if the owner wants one.
  - **What it doesn't change:** production's turn prompt reads neither the chapter question nor the kind of milestone (the framed chapter turn, B2, is held for the owner), so the change reaches play through the planner's own milestones and steps, and the pages' context. `chapterFull` (turn round 1's framed turn) still prints the story's switch/thread instructions at its end (`turnRound1Turns.ts`); the reruns phase should build its form without them (fix 2).
- **Fix 2, the chapter rules for the planners only.** Production's switch turn no longer prints SPECIAL SWITCH/THREAD INSTRUCTIONS (the story's thread types and switch/thread instructions, `BeatPromptService.getSectionsForContext`); it was the only turn that did. The two planners keep them. The switch turn still reads each stat's "Adjustments after threads" and the line telling it to consider them, which is where after-chapter stat changes come from. Unmeasured, as the coordinator settled; the byte-for-byte tests take it as a logged turn delta (`withoutChapterRules` in `tests/helpers/adoptedDeltas.ts`: the measured switch turn minus its last section), updated deliberately.
  - **The setup form, checked, unchanged:** production's chapter-rules field (`setupSchema.ts`, `instructionsText`, on every custom story, template and AI Iteration that writes the field) already says "Stat changes after threads belong in each stat's adjustments after threads", and that the planners apply the rules "while the beats that narrate a thread never see these rules"; the engine block says the beat after each thread applies each stat's adjustments. So no sentence was added, no setup variant was built, and the setup retest has nothing to measure for this fix. The field's "the beats that narrate a thread never see these rules" is now true of every turn.
  - **Left for the reruns:** the eval's turn forms keep the rules as they ran (turnB6, prod, round 2's forms on switch turns, and `chapterFull` on chapter steps), so a rerun of a switch-turn or framed-turn form should drop them in a new variant.
- **Fix 3, the turn pages' context** (`ratingContext.ts`, `chapterTextSections`): above the turn being judged, the switch turn that opened the chapter and the chapter's earlier turns in full (title, paragraphs as the player saw them with pictures as notes, the choice and its result), per player in order, one open fold each; earlier chapters stay summaries in the folded "Story so far" (which still lists the current chapter's turns too, so its chronology stays whole). The chapter rules show only in the folded "The story", as "Chapter rules (for the planners, not the turns)".
  - **Which chapter, my call:** a chapter step reads its own chapter; a switch after a chapter and the ending read the chapter that just ended (they follow its last turn and narrate its result, so that is where continuity lives); a chain item's opening reads its opening switch. The first turn shows none.
  - **The label, my call:** "for the planners, not the turns" rather than "used by the planners only", because the older pages' switch-turn options were written from prompts that did carry the rules; the label tells the rater not to hold a turn to them, which is true on every page.
  - **Re-rendered** from their keys, no API calls: `turns-r2.html` (07a4ff220b) and the older `round1-turns.html` (5e2a3f7862), `round2-turns.html` (891345004e) and `round3-turns.html` (c4867b2d79); the pages before are in `rating/superseded/` as `*-before-chapter-text.html`; each passed the leak check. **Proof:** page id and set id in the page data, every item id, and every rating input's item, label and field (144, 298, 244 and 100 inputs; the tuples' hashes match old and new) are unchanged; `turns-r2.html` is identical outside the context lines; every context equals the old one once the 42, 36, 31 and 25 added chapter-text folds are taken out and the chapter-rules label reverted, the older pages also after taking out the 22, 18 and 20 backfilled chapter question and plan lines (the context change of 2026-09-27, which those pages were never re-rendered with). Outside the context the older pages carry two rendering changes from after their last render: the pairwise controls' style and script (their saved ranks and verdicts read as before) and the ending items' options and interludes left out, as the game shows an ending (turn doc M21). `turns-r1.html` was not re-rendered: the reruns rebuild it with new outputs.
  - **No browser check:** ToolSearch finds no browser tool (the Playwright server failed to connect); `ratingContext.test.ts` covers the folds, their order, headings, open state, the pictures and the blinding labels.
  - **The older pages now show the stored chapters' backfilled questions,** the same frames the owner flagged; 8 of 21 restate their outcome by the new check. They are context shared by both options; re-framing them would need a new backfill (money), not in this change.
- **Fix 4, the budget:** the hard cap $33 → $40 (the owner's approval of 2026-09-28; `HARD_CEILING`, its tests, the dry run's total line and the context doc), and a ledger stage per run of this workflow with the coordinator's caps and a recorded reason each: `plan-refresh` $0.10, `reruns` $0.60, `setup-retests` $0.10, `groups` $0.40, `form-gate` $0.40, `final-check` $0.60 ($2.20). The ledger reads $31.99; with the stalled Stage 4 calls' possible $1.3 on top (`UNRECORDED_STAGE4_USD`, named on the dry run's total line) the six caps reach $35.49, inside $40. **My calls:** each run is its own `--run --stage` (`FEEDBACK_STAGES` in `arms.ts`), reading against stored references with no baseline like the rounds, so a cap stop in one run can't eat another's money and the spend reads per run; the stages plan no arms yet, since each run's phase decides what it runs (the dry run prints their rows at 0 jobs). The prep modes (`--judge-records`, `--backfill-chapters`, `--judge-calibration`, `--setup-chain`) still book to `turn-rounds` (spent) and `setup-rounds` ($0.35 left); a run that needs judged checks under its own cap routes them first. No cap was raised with `--stage-cap`; nothing was spent.

## Verification fixes on the owner's feedback (implementer, 2026-09-28)

Four verified findings on fixes 1 to 4, one commit, test-first. No API call.

- **The topic-switch clause of the chapter question (planner v2c and production).** The question field narrowed a flavor switch's question but still said "After a topic switch: the chosen direction, asked as a question", which asks the outcome's question again whenever the direction restates it (the Arielle chapter's pick was "Investigate the operations and key players of the Clandestine Waste Ring to expose corruption"; planner v2 restated the outcome on three of the six topic-switch planning cases). It now reads "the chosen direction, narrowed to a question about this thread's own situation, even where the direction restates its outcome", in `turnRound1Planners.ts` (`NEARER_QUESTION_FIELD`) and `plannerReplies.ts` (`QUESTION`). **Edited in place, my call:** planV2c has no stored call yet (none in `calls.jsonl`), so this is still its first text, not a new variant; planV2b's field stays as it ran. The byte-for-byte test (`adoptedPlanners.test.ts`) passes unchanged since both sides moved together; it gained a deliberate assertion that production carries the narrowed clause and not "asked as a question". `chapterFrames.ts`'s backfill keeps its old field: its 19 frames are stored and measured.
- **The kind-of-milestone check on planner v2 plans.** Planner v2 and v2b write no kind of milestone, so their stored kind is their question, and `milestoneKindConcrete` was reading questions (the "90% ±11" and chains "90%" baselines above). Now a stored kind that is the thread's own question is a copy and is not read, and where the reply text is stored `checksForRecords` passes the reply as written to `checkThreadDesign` (`asWritten`), which reads the written `typeOfMilestone` before the fallback: planner v2c's blank kind fails instead of passing on its question; a reply that writes none (v2, v2b) has no reading. Re-run `--check-baselines` over the same 4,003 replies: the planner v2 rows' kind column is now "–"; the other kind and question figures the log quotes read as before (today's form 84% ±11, in chains 87% ±5; gpt-4.1-mini 20-33%; planner v2's questions 76% ±6 and 69%; stored chapters 13 and 6 of 21). Today's form, and the stored chapters it wrote, are planV2c's only kind references. The two question-shaped "generic kind" inputs in `turnDesignChecks.test.ts` became kind-shaped phrases.
- **The plan-refresh reason** said "38 plans a sample"; there are 19 thread cases, so it now says "19 plans a sample (planner v2's 38 plans at two samples came to $0.049)". The $0.10 cap stands.
- **A stale production comment** in `ThreadPromptService.ts` still said switch beats need the chapter rules for stat changes after threads; it now says the rules are for the two planners only and the switch turn applies each stat's own "Adjustments after threads", as `BeatPromptService.ts` says.

## The owner's feedback: the missing steps (implementer, 2026-09-28)

The four paid runs after the owner's feedback (plan refresh, reruns, the rebuilt page, setup retests), each in its own feedback stage. Harness first, test-first, in one commit; the runs' verdicts and the adoption below.

- **The nearer backfill, my calls:** planner v2c's own question and kind-of-milestone field texts in the backfill's chapter words (`chapterWords`: thread → chapter, its beats → its turns), so the frames ask exactly what production's planner asks; the chapter's stored, generic kind of milestone is left out of the chapter shown (it is the thing being replaced; the steps and milestones stay fixed and shown). A separate file (`chapter-frames-nearer.json`) and case field (`nearerFrames`), so the first frames, and every request built on them (chapterFull, the older pages' contexts, the judge's stored calls), stay byte for byte. The fixed milestones are today's form's, often outcome-sized ("The group uncovers concrete evidence linking key figures…"), which limits how near a question they can answer: the Arielle chapter's reads "Can Arielle turn what she learns about the Waste Ring's hidden network into evidence strong enough to expose its key insiders?".
- **Planner v2c's reference, my call:** planner v2b never ran alone, and its chapter requests are planner v2's byte for byte for one player. Rather than pay for 24 copies of stored requests, planner v2b ran ×2 on the 7 group cases only, and planner v2's records stand in on one player where today's code builds planner v2b's request for the case to the record's prompt hash and the same JSON schema (`standInKey`, `sameRequestAs`; the row names them). No switch case ran: planner v2c's switch planner is planner v2's.
- **The judge on the nearer frames, my call:** the reference's stored turns are re-judged on the nearer frame too (their own judge calls, `judge-v3-nearer-…`), so a concrete-progress reading compares both sides on the same chapter question; turns the new frame doesn't change (chains on their own plans) reuse their calls. The readings go to `judged-turns-nearer.md` so the rounds' file stays.
- **The framed turn, my call:** `chapterFullB` is `chapterFull` minus its closing chapter-rules section, on the nearer frames, nothing else; not combined with B6, which production has on rolled chapter steps, because the page asks about B2 (adopting B2 would put the two together and needs its own byte-for-byte test).
- **Today's pair beside the chains, my call:** sample 3 of today's planner-into-turn chains on the single-player chapter cases, after the candidate on each case, so the chapter openings' wait reads against a run at the same hour (the stored references ran at 7 ms a token; this evening's turns ran 52-55 s a step). Chapter steps read on output tokens against the stored references, as round 1 did.
- **The page:** turns-r1b takes the old page's 10 chapter-step cases (`--cases`) and its 4 chapter-opening cases (`--chain-cases`, new), a fresh salt, and the nearer frames in the context (`--frames nearer`, kept in the key). The old page and key move to `rating/superseded/` and `keys/superseded/`; `readKey` now looks there when `keys/` has no match, so an export from the old page still scores (the Stage 4 v1 note in the context doc said to move keys back by hand).
- **The setup retests, my calls:** the kids examples become "taken from this story's own world (a picnic story might count Snacks, a pirate story Gold Coins)", two story kinds neither kids premise is, so a copied example would show; the identity clause loses "and stats" and nothing else. Fix 2 added no after-chapter sentence, so there is none to retest. The retest premises are the five that name their player characters or are read with a child; the sanity pass is the other 13 at one sample, cut first by the cap. A new check, `noPlayerNamedStats`, reads per-player named stats that `noSlotNames` lets pass for premise names (S8).
- **A chain reference set apart, found after the chains ran:** a chain's reference chains its sides' references, and planner v2c's is planner v2b, which never ran in a chain, so `results.md` had no row for planner v2c into the framed turn. `CHAIN_REFERENCES` in `arms.ts` sets it to today's pair (as turn round 1's planner v2 chain read), with turn round 1's chain as its second reference; `--results` re-rendered it, no calls.
