- **The verdicts** (the owner's report: `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-28_reruns-report.md`):
  - Plan refresh ($0.081 of $0.10): the nearer frames pass both chapter checks on 21 of 21 threads (the first backfill 13 and 6 of 21). Planner v2c against v2b (38 pairs, 24 of them planner v2's stand-ins): the question check moved 69% → 100% (p < 0.001), nothing moved lower; its kind of milestone 92% against today's form's 84% ±11, within the noise. Beyond the noise, not moved: the Neonate recipe's length 1 of 2 (planner v2: 4 of 4), a contest's steps naming both sides 3 of 4, one group plan repaired (a player in two threads). Production already carries planner v2c (fix 1), so it stays.
  - Reruns ($0.460 of $0.60): the framed turn without the chapter rules on the nearer frames moved none of the automatic checks against today's form or round 1's framed turn; the judge on the nearer frames reads concrete progress 78% → 91% (68 matched turns, 4-point noise, p 0.028), a reading only (the judge is not calibrated for it). Chapter openings in chains: p95 66.9 s against today's pair's 66.8 s rerun in the same half hour (10.5 ms a token), so "no slower than today's form" holds and "under 60 s" does not at this hour. Held for the owner's rating, as before.
  - Setup retests ($0.087 of $0.10): **the kids examples pass** (a kids setup copying an example 4 of 4 → 1 of 4, p 0.071, the reference's two samples level; "Courage" 4 → 1, the one copy is "Shared Snacks") and are adopted; **the identity clause does not** (Casablanca's per-player stats 2 of 2 → 1 of 2; Susan's named stats 2 of 2 → 2 of 2) and stays held. Against setupR3 on all 15 pairs nothing moved; the sanity pass was five premises (1p ×2, 2p competitive and cooperative-competitive, 3p competitive), all the stage had room for.
- **The adoption, test-first:** production's `KIDS_STATS` (`prompts/setupPromptText.ts`) takes setupR3b's line; the byte-for-byte setup tests take it as a logged delta (`KIDS_EXAMPLES` in `tests/helpers/adoptedDeltas.ts`), updated deliberately, with an assertion that the identity clause keeps "in outcomes and stats".

## The owner's feedback: group turns (B10) and the request form's gate (B9) (implementer, 2026-09-28)

The last two missing steps, each in its own feedback stage (`groups`, `form-gate`), harness test-first. Paid calls 21:29-21:56 UTC, every one valid on its first attempt; $0.56 in all, ledger $33.18 of $40 (about $34.48 with the stalled Stage 4 calls). Nothing adopted; production unchanged. The owner's report: `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-28_groups-and-form-report.md`.

- **B10 as built, my calls:** the turn doc's Appendix A note replaces today's coordination question in the prompt's coordination step and the note field's description (`schema.extend`, which keeps the field's place), on the round0 form so production could build it byte for byte with the logged deltas; the step's two examples stay (they illustrate the note's second part); "other beats" and its block stay, as turn round 3 found (and the coordinator's brief said). No line was added to the text rules: the note is written before the players' turns, so its word-for-word moment reaches them through the reply's order.
- **Today's group form's sample 2 beside it** (`GROUPS_REFERENCE_SAMPLE`): sample 1 is the migration check's (27 September), so sample 2 gives the reference its noise and a run in the candidate's half hour for the waits.
- **The judged consistency check, my calls:** a module of its own (`groupJudge.ts`), since the round judge reads one player's turn and this one needs every player's: `sharedMomentsMatch` (turn round 3's hand-read criterion: the same lines by the same speakers; paraphrase counts as no in the question, partial in calibration) and `sharedFactsAgree` (turn round 3 read 12 of 12, so a guard, uncalibratable). **The calibration set:** my own hand read of today's 12 stored group turns, written into `GROUP_JUDGE_CALIBRATION` before the judge ran (3 yes, 6 no, 3 partial; the same split as turn round 3's reader, the same six "no" cases). The judge agreed 7 of 9, failed all six hand no and passed one of three hand yes; on a re-read its two false fails are defensible under the question's own wording (a notebook open in one turn and shut in the other; a line one turn leaves out where its character is present). **Kept as recorded**, not revised after reading the evidence: it reads stricter than the hand, never passes a turn the hand fails, and is not reliable by `isReliable` (a reading, a floor). Its calibration items are the reference's sample 1, judged twice; every other reply once.
- **B10's result and its one fix-and-retest (B10b), my call:** B10 moved the judged shared moments 13% → 25% (p 0.23, not moved) and moved the closes that "wait" the wrong way (19% → 37% of closing sentences, p 0.04): the turns repeat one shared closing line for every player. The judge's evidence showed why the target barely moved: the one planned moment reads alike, the rest of the scene still drifts (a line changing speaker, "First day?" asked by Jasper in one turn and by Emil in the other). One retest, as the rounds allow: the note's first part becomes the shared moment's script (at most four lines as Speaker: "words", given alike in every turn, nothing more said aloud there, "you say" in the speaker's own turn), the facts include what is in whose hands, and a fourth part sends each turn to its own player's part to close. $0.105, inside the stage (its recorded reason now names it). B10b moved the target 13% → 58% (p 0.001) and moved two existing checks the wrong way: speech in a first turn's first paragraph (7 of 12 → 1 of 12 replies, p 0.014: the script moment comes after a scene-setting paragraph) and the sentence rule (23 of 24 → 19 of 24, p 0.094). **Neither passes the stop rule; not adopted.** B10b is where B5 was: its own target moved, existing checks read worse, so the owner's rating decides. No second retest.
- **The page, my call:** today's group form against B10b, not B10: B10b is the version that does what B10 is for, and B10 moved nothing of its own; a second page would cost the owner another 40 minutes for a form that failed its target. 11 group turns (`--items 11`, so the twelfth hosts the control), the repeat, `--criteria groups` (new: the group questions, the first one B10's own); `rating/turns-groups.html`, key `keys/text-turn-654ab04cb7.json`, export `ratings-text-turn-654ab04cb7.json`. Leak check and a scan clean. **No browser check** (the Playwright server failed to connect).
- **B9 on production's own form, my calls:** the coordinator's brief put B9 on production's current single-player turn form, so a new variant (`adoptedSplit`, `requestFormB9.ts`) splits `beatStep.request` the way turn round 3 split the round-2 form (its constants and splitter exported, its own stored requests re-checked byte for byte); production's form has no flavor opening, and a switch turn's results line is in every switch turn, so neither moves. **Production's limits on the reference too:** the `adopted` variant now carries `productionCallLimits` for its role and player count, since production sends them, so the gate compares the request shapes alone. **A new prompt state** `adopted1` (`ADOPTED_PROMPT_STATE`): records of production's own code after the adoption and the feedback fixes; the final check can use it. **One sample each** (88 turns at about $0.0033 filled most of the $0.40), in one invocation, one message first then split (arm-major order; they overlapped 21:45-21:51 and ran to 21:56). **The noise:** production's one message has one sample, so its noise comes from today's form's two stored samples on the same cases (`noiseReferenceKey`, `adopted` → `prod`; the row says so), since production's form is today's form with B6 and the logged deltas.
- **B9's verdict:** chapter-step p95 54.9 s against 55.2 s for the one message (27 steps each, 9.5 and 10.3 ms a token), median 42.5 s against 46.9 s: no worse, but over 45 s, so **the gate fails on its absolute half and B9 is not adopted**; production keeps one message. Nothing moved the wrong way (fewer repairs: clean 84% → 98%, p 0.029); billed $0.0027 against $0.0033 a turn (−18%; input −46%, 55% read from 7 cache lines); output −4%, reasoning +1% (Stage 4's split had +30-44%); no reply cut or retried. At the baseline evening's 7.2 ms a token both would read about 39-42 s (arithmetic). **My recommendation to the owner:** re-measure B9 in the paid final check at a normal-pace hour (about $0.12 inside its $0.60) and adopt it if its chapter-step p95 is at or under 45 s there; the other options are reading the gate as "no slower than the one message" and adopting now, or dropping it.
- **A free reading:** production's one-message runs are the first paid run of production's own single-player turns; against today's stored form only B6's intended moves appear (fewer levers, levers following the rate line, negative bases on bonus options), nothing else moved. The planners and group turns are not in it.
- **Spend:** `groups` $0.292 (B10 $0.101, today's group form's sample 2 $0.050, B10b $0.105, the group judge 85 calls $0.035), `form-gate` $0.268 (one message $0.147, B9 $0.121). No cap raised or passed.

## The owner's feedback: the paid final check (implementer, 2026-09-28)

- **Scope, my calls:** the coordinator's list (the 44 stored single-player turns, both planners, the 12 group turns, 6 new setups, 2 template setups on Sol low) plus the chapter-opening chains per player count, since the 60 s chapter-opening gate needs a measured planner-and-turn wait (summed p95s read high). **Not B9's re-measure:** the owner has not picked (a), (b) or (c), and tonight's pace (about 10 ms a token) would have failed its 45 s half again. **No judged checks:** the judge reads one prompt state per invocation and rewrites the rounds' file, so production's turns would have had no reference beside them.
- **Prompt state and samples:** `adopted1`, because production's code is the form gate's (nothing but docs changed since `b045e14`). The single-player turns run as sample 2 (`FINAL_CHECK_TURN_SAMPLE`), so production's own form gets its two-sample noise and a second pace reading; everything else once.
- **Models:** every final-check arm comes from `TEXT_MODEL_GROUPS` (`adoptedDefault`), so the check follows production's defaults, not a copy (a test holds it).
- **The template editor's request:** a new variant `adoptedTemplate` (production's `generateTemplateSetup`: `setupStep`'s template kind, no kids budget, the template editor's limits), rather than a flag on `adopted`, so the arm key says which request ran. It reads against Sol low's stored custom-story setups on today's form: no template form ever ran in the eval. `checkSetup`'s `difficultyModifier` now reads a template's three to five levels. `adoptedForms.test.ts` holds it to setup round 3's template form on the 18 frozen setup cases. Templates on Avalon (one player) and the soul flat (two players, cooperative-competitive, where the editor offers a contest); no kids premise, since templates have no category.
- **Stored chain references (harness fix):** `rebuiltToday` never counted a chain's turn step from another prompt state (its request depends on the stored plan), so production's chains had no reference in `results.md`. It now rebuilds the turn from the chain's own stored plan and needs the planner step current too; test-first. The re-render changed no earlier reading: the diff adds only the two final-check chain rows.
- **The run:** a 7-call smoke (one of each kind, one Sol draft among them), then 103 isolated calls and 19 chains, 22:17-22:34 UTC; 148 calls, all valid on the first attempt, no hang, no cut, no padding, no blank item. $0.561 of the $0.60 stage; the ledger $33.74 of $40.

Chapter plans: nearer question 18 of 18 (72% on planner v2, moved), concrete kind 18 of 19 (the miss a word-list false alarm: "whether Aria advances Mason and Eliot together to the next stage").
- **Watch items (beyond the noise, not moved):** group first turns, the only group turns whose request changed (fix 2, no chapter rules on a switch turn; first measured for groups here), broke the sentence rule on 3 of 6 (100% → 75% of the 12, p 0.11), each a six-sentence dialogue paragraph with names spoken as fragments; "Energy" named in 2 of 6 setups (round 3: none on these premises); the Neonate recipe missed on one isolated plan; one three-player group plan in a chain needed the game's repair (the plan refresh's same case) and its turn ran to 76 s. B6's balance on production's own options (`balance-sim-adopted1.md`, `balance-sim.md` kept as it was): favorable 42.8% against 44.7%, a strength option best outright in 72% of sets against 49%.
- **Waits:** over at tonight's pace (Luna medium 9.4-10.3 ms a token): chapter steps 57.2 s, switch turns 55.5 s summed, single-player chapter openings 60.2 s over 12 chains (today's pair 66.8-68.6 s at 9.3-10.5 ms); groups within 60 s but for that one three-player chain. Output tokens against today's form: chapter steps +6%, openings +14%. Sol's two AI Drafts took 104 and 134 s against the editor's 150 s timeout at 20-24 ms a token (10-11 on 26 September).
- **Recommendation to the owner, not acted on:** raise the template editor's timeout from 150 s to 240 s (no player waits on it; at tonight's Sol pace its longest measured setup would be cut off). A production change the owner has not asked for.

## The template editor's timeout (implementer, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided by the coordinator** on the final status note's recommendation: `productionCallLimits` gives `templateGeneration` and `templateIteration` 240 s (was 150 s), test-first (`chatModel.test.ts`, and the `adoptedTemplate` variant's limits in `executor.test.ts` and `jobPlan.test.ts`, which read the same function). No calls.
- **The output cap stays 20,000:** Sol low's longest measured setup is 8,258 tokens (9,610 at any effort, Sol medium), the two AI Drafts 5,232 and 5,661, so the cap is 2.4× (2.1×). At the slow evening's 20-24 ms a token, 8,258 tokens take about 195 s, which 150 s would have cut and 240 s does not; at that pace the timeout binds before the cap (about 10,000-12,000 tokens in 240 s).
- **What it costs an author:** a draft stuck on every attempt now errors after about 12 minutes (three attempts) instead of 7.5. The client's AI Draft and AI Iteration requests set no timeout of their own and the server none on its routes, so nothing else cuts in first.
- **Records:** the final check's two AI Drafts ran at 150 s; `adoptedTemplate` now carries 240 s, and limits are not part of the prompt hash, so the stored records still count.

## The Casablanca sentence (implementer, 2026-09-29)

- **The variant, my calls:** `setupR3c` (`ROUND3C_PARTS`) is setup round 3 with round 3b's identity clause ("use those names in outcomes", since round 3's "in outcomes and stats" asks for the opposite) and, closing the clause, "Player stats never carry a player character's name, even one the premise gives." (`PLAYER_STATS_NAMELESS`), in the one- and the multi-player clause alike, as the note's sentence reads; plus the kids examples production took from round 3b, so it is production's measured text with the one change. Stated once; in multiplayer it sits beside round 1c's "none is written for one role or named after one player" in the stats section, which it extends to the premise's own names (a partial overlap, kept: folding it into round 1c's sentence would leave the one-player clause without it). Read against `setupR3`, with `setupR3b` and `prod` second; harness test-first (the byte-for-byte test against round 3 with the two passages swapped back, AI Iteration included).
- **The second premise, my call:** Susan, not the Okafor siblings: Susan is the other named premise whose player stats carried a name on round 3 and round 3b (2 of 2 on both), so the target check could move under the stop rule; the Okafor siblings and the stuffed animals were clean on both forms (checked in their stored outputs), and with Casablanca alone 2 of 2 → 0 of 2 is p 0.17, which can never move.
- **The run** (setup-rounds, `round0`, 23:18-23:23 UTC on 2026-09-28): dry run est $0.019 (by hand from round 3b's setups $0.021-0.023); a smoke of Casablanca s1, whose sent clause I read in its stored prompt, then the other three; all four valid on the first attempt; $0.023; setup-rounds $2.67 of $3, the ledger $33.76 of $40.
- **The reading:** Casablanca no longer writes a set of player stats per named player (0 of 2; round 3 2 of 2 with six player stats, round 3b 1 of 2): "Social Ease, Emotional Energy, Trusted Supports, Personal Grounding" and "Emotional Bandwidth, Trusted Confidants, Family Trust, Personal Anchors"; the scoreboards ("Fatima's Voice|Layla's Voice", "Fatima's Hope|Layla's Hope") still name them, as the scoreboard line allows. Susan's player stats carry her name in 2 of 2 (all three in one, "Susan's Keepsakes" in the other), as on both earlier forms. Against `setupR3` over the four pairs: nothing moved lower; `implicationsPerStatMean` moved higher (1.46 → 1.73, 2.1 SE); `noPlayerNamedStats` 0% → 50% and `playerStats` 50% → 100% beyond the noise, not moved (p 0.214 each); also beyond the noise, not moved: `threeOutcomes` 100% → 75% (one Casablanca setup wrote two shared and two personal outcomes per player). Against `setupR3b` nothing moved.
- **Verdict: does not pass; production unchanged.** `adoptedSetup.test.ts` now asserts production sends the sentence on no player count. The eval keeps the variant and its records.
- **What would pass, not run (outside the brief):** the sentence in the multiplayer clause only (it did nothing for Susan, and a single player's stat named after the one character is harmless), with Casablanca at six samples on both round 3 and the new form (6 of 6 → 0 of 6 would be p 0.001): four more round 3 setups and six new ones, about $0.065, inside the setup-rounds stage's $0.33.

## Rating pages: option mechanics and each turn's changes (implementer, 2026-09-29)

- **The owner's feedback:** "options should also display the stat changes that come along with it. Not resolving a sacrificed resource etc. is one of the worst mistakes. Badly adjusting other stats can be an issue, too. Cannot rate that right now."
- **The coordinator's design, built test-first** (`ratingMechanics.ts`; `ratingMechanics.test.ts` and a block in `rating.test.ts`): under each version's options, per player, each choice's mechanics as the game plays it; after them, what the turn changes after the game's repairs, with each stat change as before → after on the case's state (the game's own `ChangeService`), a line on the previous choice's sacrifice or reward, and a flag on a change that doesn't fit its stat. `.context/text-model-eval.md` has the detail. No call, no spend.
- **Choices I made inside the design:**
  - A lever's stat is not a field of the option, so it is read from the text as `sacrificeApplied` reads it (the stat named), then the stat whose held value it names (an item or trait), then the one stat allowing that lever. The amount is the number the text names; the stat's own rule prints beside it.
  - "Applied" also reads the direction: a sacrifice lowers or removes, a reward raises or adds (opposites and plain strings: any change). A lever on a stat that changes only after chapters is exempt from that flag.
  - A bonus on an id the game can't resolve is shown the way the game shows it, by the id, since the points still count.
  - Plain text, no badges or colours on the flags, so the lines read as content, not as a verdict. No stylesheet or instruction change, so a page differs from its old self only by the new rows.
  - `metadataLeaks` now reads a turn option's own line labels (the chain plan's, the mechanics', the changes'), which it didn't before.
- **The pages, re-rendered from their keys:** `turns-r1b`, `turns-r2`, `turns-groups` and the three older round pages. Old copies are in `rating/superseded/*-before-mechanics.html`. Checked by a temporary Jest script, since deleted: on all six, page data, item ids and input tuples are identical, and each page is byte-identical to its old copy once the new rows are removed. No browser check (no Playwright tool in the session).
- **Counts for the owner** (all stored single-player turns of the live pages' arms, every sample, replies after repairs):
  - A previous sacrifice or reward was applied every time: today's form 46 of 46, `chapterFullB` 20 of 20, `turnR2b` 24 of 24, today's chains 3 of 3, planner v2c's chains 2 of 2 (production's own form, the final check's `adopted1`: 24 of 24). None went the wrong way or was written without effect. Each paid the amount its text named, except one `turnR2b` turn: -20 on Personal Agency against the text's "-10%".
  - A stat change that doesn't fit its stat: 1 of 104 on today's form (a string[] Public Sentiment item added during a chapter, a stat that changes only after one), 0 of 20, 0 of 79, 0 of 29 and 0 of 3 on the others (production 0 of 66). The flags on the older round pages come from gpt-4.1-mini's turns (off-ladder values, a setString on a list).
  - **A side finding, production-visible:** 14-20% of the counted stat bonuses (today's form 84 of 524, production 35 of 258) name a player stat in the doubled seat form (`player1_player_skills_traits`). `Story.getStatById` doesn't resolve it, so the game counts the points but shows the raw id in the roll breakdown the player sees (`PreviousChoiceVisualizer`). `beatRepairs` already maps that form for stat changes, not for bonuses. Not fixed here: a production change for the coordinator to decide.

## The stage scoping: planner v2d and the judged stage check (implementer, 2026-09-29)

- **The owner's feedback:** the Waste Ring's first chapter ("Gathering Intel", "Infiltrating the Network", "Exposing the Corruption") on an outcome of three milestones: milestone 1 should only settle the evidence, but step 3 already moves on to exposing it.
- **Built, test-first, eval side only** (commit `395327f` and the one after it): `planV2d` (planner v2c's chapter planner with the outcome's stages), the stage `stage-scoping` ($0.40, reason recorded), the built case `round-thread-first-8988006e-t1`, the judged check `staysWithinStage` with its calibration, and the proxies `stagesNamed` and `stepsWithinNamedStage`. `.context/text-model-eval.md` has the detail.
- **Choices I made inside the design:**
  - The stage rule is a list item before the question item, not a rewrite of the question or milestone items, so planner v2c's text stays byte for byte around it and the milestone-size rule keeps its words; PACING adds only "; this thread settles stage k of n".
  - **In the story's last thread, k stays milestones so far + 1.** An outcome still needing several milestones there gets its next stage, not its last: that is what the milestone rule's "while it needs more than one, each is one step" already says, and the ending settles the rest. Folding the stages left into the last thread would contradict that rule; a question for the owner if the climax should settle the outcome instead.
  - The reply names the stages (`outcomeStages`) before the question, so the question is asked inside the stage; code, not the model, computes k.
  - The worked example stays the prompt's own (Rikkit, the noble's letters, stages "prove the noble's hand; turn the Guild against him; stop the conspiracy"), so no stored story's names reach the prompt and its stage 1 is the question item's existing good question.
  - The judge names the stages itself and never sees a plan's own list, so planner v2d cannot pass by naming convenient stages; its example is another story's.
  - Calibration includes two constructed failing versions of planner v2c plans (the audition turned into the contract signing, the clearing into saving the whole forest), because every stored hand no is gpt-4.1-mini's: without them the judge could pass by telling the models apart.
  - The proxy reads planner v2d's own list only; no other form writes one, so it has no baseline on stored plans.
- **Read before paying:** the built requests end to end for a first chapter (the built case, 0 of 3), a middle chapter (`thread-8988006e-t5-o0`, 1 of 3), a last chapter (the same with 2 of 3 recorded) and a late binding chapter (the same with 4 turns left, the story's last thread, 1 of 3), plus a three-player chapter. No contradiction with the pacing block, the nearer-question rule, the outcome kinds or the one-milestone rule; the late chapter reads as the story's climax on stage 2 of 3, as decided above. The built case shows one Waste Ring fact from the chapter's first step ("initial intelligence gathering difficult and risky"), the cut-back's known limit.
- **The run did not happen:** the stage judge's smoke came back 429 `credit_balance_exhausted` on every attempt (18:27 UTC on and again later): the OpenAI account has no credit. $0 billed. Nothing was paid, nothing adopted, production keeps planner v2c.
  - **Harness fix, test-first:** such a 429 is no longer retried and leaves its job open (`NEVER_RETRY_CODES`, `finishesJob`), so the next invocation sends it once credit is back; before, three retries each and the job read as finished. `--judge-stages --cases` runs a smoke.
- **A free hand reading (mine, not the judge's):** planner v2c's stored plans stay within their stage on about 28 to 30 of the 30 where the check applies (two borderline: a band's first club set carrying its name "across Mars"); gpt-4.1-mini on today's form reaches past it on about 6 of 24 (contracts signed, overlords made dominant, the forest's future secured, all at stage 1 of 3); the stored chapters in play fail on both Novi Reg first chapters. So the owner's chapter is gpt-4.1-mini's plan from the stored story, and on the stored cases the stop rule may have nothing to move; the built first chapter, where no GPT-6 planner has run, is the test that matters.
- **Next, once the account has credit** (about $0.15-0.20 of the stage's $0.40): the judge's smoke, its calibration with the baseline arms, a three-job plan smoke on the built case (compare with the dry run's count), the 44-job run, the judge on planner v2d, the stop rule. If planner v2c stays within stage on the stored cases, more samples of the built case (both arms, as the Casablanca retest did) are what could move the check.
- **Spend:** $0; the ledger $33.85 of $40.

## Verification fixes: the lever's stat and the stage scoping's case (implementer, 2026-09-29)

- **A lever text naming two stats could hide an unpaid sacrifice** (`ratingMechanics.ts`): the reading took the first named stat, in the state's order, that changed at all, and said "not applied" only when none did, so "Burn 10 Supplies to steady your Nerve" with only Nerve raised read "Nerve: changed the other way". Fixed test-first: `leverStat` picks the one stat the lever pays (of the named stats whose rule allows that lever, the one named nearest an amount the text names, else the first named) and the reading reads that stat alone ("Supplies: not applied"); the option's own line names it too, with its rule. Any stat the text names stays exempt from the after-a-chapter flag, since a turn may touch it.
  - **Nothing on the pages moved:** all six turn pages re-rendered from their keys came out byte-identical to the handed-out copies, and the counts over every stored single-player turn are the ones above (the 11 distinct previous-lever texts each name one stat).
- **The stage scoping's built case reopened closed stages' jobs** (`casesFor`): frozen as a round case after turn-rounds, migration and the feedback stages before stage-scoping had closed, it added 18 open jobs to their dry-run rows (turn-rounds 2 + 4 chains, migration 2 + 1, plan-refresh 2, reruns 5 chains, final-check 1 + 1). Fixed test-first: `CASE_FIRST_STAGE` and `stagePlansCase` (`arms.ts`) plan a case frozen for a later stage only from that stage on, in `STAGES` order; the dry run for the case now reads 0 jobs in every stage but stage-scoping, which keeps its 6.
- **The stage scoping, tried again after the fixes:** the judge's smoke (`--judge-stages --prompt-state round0 --cases owner-waste-ring,agency-luna-s1`, 4 calls, est $0.003) came back 429 `credit_balance_exhausted` on all four at 19:10 UTC, $0 billed; the jobs stay open. The run still waits on credit; production keeps planner v2c. The smoke warned that the judge's prompt changed since the first refused attempts: that is commit `2470e38`'s later-stages wording, and those attempts never finished.
- **Spend:** $0.

## Round 0 play fixes: stat bonuses name their stat in the form the game resolves (implementer, 2026-09-29)

- **The finding** (the rating pages' side finding above): 14-20% of counted stat bonuses in stored GPT-6 turns wrote the stat's id doubled (`player1_player_skills_traits`). `Story.getStatById` doesn't resolve it, so the points counted but the roll breakdown players see (`PreviousChoiceVisualizer`) showed the raw id.
- **Fixed test-first** (`beatRepairs.ts`, `repairBonuses`, after the option-type repair, on every challenge option, the ending's too): an id `getStatById` already resolves is kept; one it doesn't is read as the stat-change repair reads a seat form (`playerStatFromSeatForm`) and written as `player1_skills_traits` (`bonusStatIdSeatForm`). Now the breakdown reads "+15 Skills & Traits" where it read "+15 player1_player_skills_traits". Tests in `beatRepairs.test.ts` (doubled, doubled without a seat, the plain seat form, the exact id, a shared stat, a stat id without `player_`, unknown, ambiguous, a seat the story lacks, single player, the ending, the breakdown end to end, the log line), `ratingMechanics.test.ts` and `outputChecks.test.ts`.
- **Choices I made:**
  - **The seat form the bonus contract asks for** ("replace 'player' with the player slot id"), not the plain id the stat-change repair writes: a repaired bonus then reads like a compliant one wherever ids are compared (B6's whole-step stat in `balanceSim.ts`), and it keeps the seat. The plain id only where the stat's id has no `player_` to replace, the only form the lookup resolves there. A guard falls back to the plain id should the seat form resolve to another stat.
  - **What the lookup already resolves is not a repair**, the plain seat form included, so a compliant reply still passes `noRepairs`.
  - **Ambiguity follows the stat-change rule, with the option's owner as the group:** in multiplayer another player's seat is ambiguous (`bonusStatAmbiguous`) and a seat the story lacks is unknown (`bonusStatUnknown`); in single player every seat is the one player's. Neither occurs in the stored turns.
  - **Unresolved ids stay as written and are notes, not drops:** the game counts a bonus's points whatever its id, so dropping one would change the odds.
  - **Not covered:** a shared stat with a seat in front (`player1_shared_cafe_reputation`, 3 bonuses in all stored turns, none on today's form or production) and a doubled shared id (`shared_shared_city_surveillance`, 1). The stat-change repair doesn't read those either; they stay `bonusStatUnknown` notes.
  - **Running stories:** beats stored before the deploy keep their ids, so the next roll on such a beat can still show a raw id once; rolls already made keep their stored breakdown.
- **Every reader of a bonus's id sees the repaired one:** the roll and its breakdown (`BeatResolutionService.calculateTotalPoints`, on beats `generateBeats` stores repaired, pregenerated siblings included); the client never gets an id (the breakdown's names and tooltips, the bonus's reason, are computed on the server, and `ClientStateManager` strips bonuses from undecided beats); the eval's checks (`checksForRecords`; `knownIds` accepted the doubled form already), the mechanics rows (`readTurn`), B6's balance simulation, the setup chain and the round-case builder all run `repairBeatReply` first. No prompt renders a bonus's id, so no request changes; the production-equals-variant tests stay green.
- **Counts on the stored turns** (every final usable turn, isolated and chains; counted bonuses are the first two per challenge option; fixed of counted, then what stays unresolved):
  - Today's form (`round0`, Luna medium `prod`): 84 of 524 fixed, 0 left. Production (`adopted1`, Luna medium `adopted`): 35 of 258, 0 left; `adoptedSplit` 19 of 130.
  - The rounds' single-player arms: `chapterSlim` 66 of 292, `chapterFull` 36 of 277, `chapterSlimPlans` 42 of 277, `chapterFullB` 59 of 296, `turnR2b` 34 of 249, `turnR2Paragraphs` 18 of 125, `turnR2c` 14 of 100, `turnR2` 2 of 9; their chains 5 to 14 of 90 to 143 each, production's chain 2 of 41; 0 left on all.
  - Groups: `turnB10` 12 of 106, `turnB10b` 6 of 98, Luna low `prod` 0 of 89 (`round0`) and 0 of 51 (`adopted1`); today's group chain 6 of 96 with 3 unknown left, the other group chains 0 of 59 and 0 of 100.
  - The `postfix` GPT-6 arms: 9 to 90 fixed per isolated arm (at most 2 unknown left), 0 to 12 per chain; Sol low 0 of 55.
  - gpt-4.1-mini: `prefix` 23 of 197 (8 unknown left), `postfix` 42 of 370 (28 left), its chains 27 of 154 (7 left), `rewrite` 13 of 168 (1 left). Its unknowns are mostly invented ids (`player1_relationship_with_staff` for `player_staff_relationship`, `player1_creative`).
- **The pages, re-rendered from their keys:** `turns-r1b` (35 bonus lines), `turns-r2` (8), `round1-turns` (16), `round2-turns` (16) and `round3-turns` (12) changed; `turns-groups` came out byte-identical, so it was left in place. Old copies are in `rating/superseded/*-before-bonus-ids.html`. Checked by a temporary Jest script, since deleted: page data, every input tag and the item ids identical; split at every tag, the only differing segments are stat-bonus lines, each a doubled seat form before. Before the change, all six re-rendered byte-identical to the handed-out copies.
- **`results.md`, re-rendered free (`--results`):** against a re-render with the code before the fix, only the repair readings move: `repair:bonusStatIdSeatForm` and `note:bonusStatUnknown` appear, and `noRepairs` reads lower on the turn arms that wrote the doubled form, by 3 to 33 points per arm (today's form, `round0` Luna medium `prod`: 91.6% → 72.5%; the group arm Luna low `prod`, which wrote none, stays 92.0%). No verdict row changed. B6's balance simulation reads the same on both arms, so `balance-sim.md` was not re-rendered. `check-baselines.md` was not re-rendered: its `noRepairs` baselines would move the same way.
- **No visual check:** the client needs no change, and Playwright failed to connect in this session.

## The stage scoping, run (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The brief:** credit is back; run the stage scoping as planned, calibrate the judged stage check to the other judged checks' bar, and build a small climax arm (only the last-chapter clause differs) for the owner's open question, run on the late and last chapters only. Planner v2d's own last-chapter clause (the next stage) is the one carried; the climax clause is not adopted either way.
- **Smoke:** one planner v2d call on the built first chapter, valid (7.5 s, $0.0012): the account answers.
- **The judge, my one fix:** v1 agreed with the hand on 20 of 21 (hand yes 10 of 11, no 10 of 10), but its two samples agreed on only 20 of 24, under the 90% bar. The four flips had two causes: three rode on how broadly the judge named a later stage that time (a result that enlarges this stage's result, "significantly boosting their fame", "forces more insiders into view", read as the next stage's), and once, on the twin, a step asking how to expose the Ring passed on its results. Prompt v2 adds one decision rule (`STAGE_DECISION_RULE`: decide by what a step asks and what a result says has happened, not by the judge's own stage names; a result that makes this stage's result bigger, or a later stage easier, stays within, however big its words), with a Mara example, not a stored story's; test-first. v2: 21 of 21 (11 of 11, 10 of 10), samples 23 of 24: reliable. The hand verdicts were left as set before either run.
- **The climax arm, my calls:** no stored or built planning case is in its story's last thread with an outcome still needing several milestones (the short story's late chapter has a complete outcome), so the clause would change no request there. Three last chapters built by editing frozen cases (`turnsLeft`, no calls): Novi Reg's second chapter after the switch's first option with 4 turns left and after its second with 2 (the Waste Ring at 1 of 3), and the three-player second chapter with 4 turns left (three outcomes at 0 of 2), the situation binding late produces when a setup plans more milestones than chapters fit. The clause is one sentence in the stage item plus PACING's folded line ("2 still needed, but this is the story's last thread, so this thread's milestone is its last one; this thread settles stages 2 and 3, the last"), so the milestone rule's "when this is its last one" applies instead of contradicting it; both print only where `foldsStages`, so the variant is planner v2d byte for byte elsewhere. Planner v2c ran twice on the last chapters too, for what production does there today. Inside stage-scoping's $0.40, its recorded reason extended, rather than a new stage. `--build-stage-cases` now leaves already-frozen cases as they are.
- **The run:** dry run 62 jobs, 61 open after the smoke; the run planned the same 62 (61 open). All 61 valid on the first attempt. Then the judge on every chapter plan of planner v2d, v2c, today's form, planner v2 and the climax arm (178 calls; the default `--max-spend 0.10` refused the $0.12 estimate, which errs high, so `--max-spend 0.15`).
- **Readings against planner v2c, the stop rule:**
  - The judged stage check: v2d 35 of 38 plans, v2c 33 of 38, within the noise. Against today's form (32 of 32) both read 29 of 32 on the shared pairs, beyond the noise, not moved (p 0.119).
  - The owner's chapter: v2d, v2c and today's form each 2 of 2 within stage 1; the failing chapter the owner read is gpt-4.1-mini's (stored chapters in play 10 of 15, both Waste Ring first chapters failing).
  - Nothing moved either way. Nearer question 100% → 100%, concrete kind 93.5% → 93.5%, pacing lengths 97.8% → 97.8%, contest sides 75% → 100% (within the noise), plans usable 100%. The switch planner is planner v2b's byte for byte, so one outcome per direction is unchanged (no switch case ran). Beyond the noise, not moved: kind follows outcome 90.9% → 95.5%, no group in single player 100% → 96.7%.
  - The proxy `stepsWithinNamedStage` reads 60.5% against the judge's 92% on the same plans (13 false flags where a later stage's name repeats a story word); `stagesNamed` 97.7%.
  - A hand count: 4 of 46 v2d plans repeat their last step (v2c 1 of 46; not moved, p about 0.18). One, on the owner's chapter (s2), also writes a stat instruction into its plan ("raise city surveillance by 5%").
- **Verdict: no regression, no gain; not adopted in this run.** Its target could not move: planner v2c already stays within its stage. Production keeps planner v2c; adopting a form whose target did not move (as the Casablanca sentence did not) is the owner's call. What v2d does change is the last chapter: 6 of 6 settle the next stage, where v2c settles the outcome outright in 1 of 6.
- **The climax arm:** 6 of 6 plans settle their outcome (judged 0 of 6 within stage, as designed); 2 of 6 repeat their last step, one a 3-beat plan for a 2-turn last chapter (`lengthAllowed` 5 of 6); reasoning tokens +63% against v2d there. The owner decides which last chapter the game should have; the data is in the stage scoping report.
- **Spend:** $0.166 in stage-scoping (plans $0.089, judge $0.077); the ledger $34.02 of $40.

## The owner's feedback of 2026-09-30: options and continuity, the build (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What was built, no calls, $0:** three eval arms on production's single-player turn form (`storyTextRounds/turnOptionsContinuity.ts`; variants `turnO`, `turnC`, `turnOC` with production's single-player turn limits), the owner's new checks (`feedbackChecks` in `turnDesignChecks.ts`, three new shares in `RATIOS`), a stage `options-continuity` (cap $1.40, reason recorded) under a new prompt state `adopted2`, interleaved. Test-first; the base is held to `beatStep.request` byte for byte on fixture stories and all 44 frozen single-player turns. Context doc updated (variants, arms, budget, checks, baselines).
- **The base, my call:** built from the frozen copy (`turnB6`) with the switch turn's chapter rules cut as `adoptedDeltas.ts` cuts them, not from `beatStep`, so a later production change can't move the arms (as every round variant); the kept test is what ties it to production.
- **Arm O's lever rule, a fix to the brief (the coordinator's call to revert):** the brief's "the first sacrifice option in a chapter as today's line allows; from the second on, the line says a sacrifice was already offered … so another is offered only when the scene gives a strong reason" reads as the second sacrifice under no rate at all. That puts a second sacrifice under a looser gate than a first (a first is refused two challenge turns after any lever; a second would not be), and on the stored turns it would have replaced today's "none this turn" on 21 of the 32 rolled chapter steps (their played chapters carry gpt-4.1-mini's sacrifices, 75% of its challenge sets), loosening production's B6 where the owner wrote sacrifices are "maybe even too common". So `chapterLeverRule` keeps today's rate as the gate for every lever and the chapter's counts only narrow it: no reward once the chapter offered one, and a sacrifice after the chapter's first only for a strong reason made clear in the option's text. Reverting is one line in `chapterLeverRule` (and its tests).
- **What that leaves measurable on the 44 stored turns:** the reward cap binds on none (no stored chapter offered a reward before its turn, and in a three-step chapter today's rate already rules out a second lever); the strong-reason clause on 3 cases (`cont-7492b211-t2-o0..o2`, whose played first step typed its options as exploration, so today's rate reads no lever there and fits, while the chapter did offer a sacrifice). Production offered a second sacrifice on 5 of those 6 turns and on 2 turns of `cont-8988006e-t2` against its own "none" line; all 7 state no reason in the scene (by hand; the heuristic agrees). Arm O's option-design line reaches all 32 rolled steps.
- **Arm O's wording, my calls:** "Each option also draws on a different stat" (not "strength": B6's second way is the one that plays to the character's strength, so "each leans on a different strength" read as a contradiction); the hedge "(as far as the stats' current values give bonuses)" so "at most one has no stat bonus" can't push a bonus the modifiers line forbids ("only if the actual, current value of the stat warrants it"); one weak example for the mechanics ("three options that each earn +10 from Nerve and differ only in how risky they are"), since B6's weak example is about words. Nothing else of B6 restated; the lever line keeps its place and its "Sacrifice or reward:" start; NO_DOUBLE_SACRIFICE stays (it is about the same stat, the line about the count).
- **Arm C, my calls:** the one instruction goes in the prompt's text rules, first, in place of "--- continue exactly where the previous beat for this player ended"; the text field's "- Start exactly where …" is cut so it is said once; "Describe in detail the action …" and the first-paragraph rules stay (the calibrated "first paragraph narrates the choice" reads them). The first turn is left as production sends it (no previous beat; production's first turn still carries the old line, a round-2 B3 item held for rating). The CONTEXT's "Each beat must flow naturally from the previous beat OF THAT PLAYER" stays: it is the multiplayer mechanics' line, the same on every turn. The previous beat's full text goes only where the state lacked it, a chapter's first step (the switch turn, raw as the state shows beat texts, before CURRENT STEP); a chapter's later steps, switch turns after a chapter and the ending already show it in full.
- **Read end to end** (production's and arm OC's requests, prompt and schema): a chapter's first step (`cont-8988006e-t5-o0`), a middle step after a sacrifice was taken (`cont-7492b211-t2-o1`, rate fitting, and `cont-8988006e-t6-o1`, rate none), a last step after a reward was offered (built in memory from `cont-8988006e-t7-o1`, step 1's sacrifice made a reward: today's rate gives none, so the line stays "none this turn", the realistic case in a three-step chapter), a switch turn (`cont-8988006e-t4-o0`) and the ending. Fixed from the read: "strength" → "stat" (above) and the lever gate (above). Checked and left: "every beat changes something" beside the step rule's "WITHOUT defining the resolution of the step" (a consequence landing or pressure shifting is not the step's answer); "nothing already narrated is narrated again" beside the switch turn's full paragraph on the milestone (the milestone event is new); the chapter line beside NO_DOUBLE_SACRIFICE (count and stat, not the same rule); production's empty "BEAT TEXTS for player1:" above the new block on a first step (production's text, left as is).
- **Checks, my calls:** the main stat is the largest counted bonus (first two, ±15, as the game counts), none without a positive one; "only risk tells them apart" is two normal options with the same counted stat set (empty included) and the same base; second sacrifices counted on any option type (the stored `7492b211` case needs it); `leverFollowsChapterLine` was built and dropped: with the rate as the gate it equals `leverFollowsRateLine` plus `atMostOneRewardPerChapter`. The reuse heuristic's thresholds (five-word sentences, an eight-word run, 75% of words) were set on the unit examples before any stored turn was read.
- **Baselines** (every stored single-player turn; the context doc has the table's headline): production (adopted1) main stats distinct 27% ±3 of 64 challenge sets (1.73 main stats a set), only-risk sets 20% ±9, second sacrifices 11% ±9, levers 23%, rewards 2 of 15 levers, reuse 81 of 82 turns clean; today's form 12%, 44%, 47%, 64%, 18%, 100%; gpt-4.1-mini 38%, 2%, 75%, 98%, 0%, 91%. The reuse flags on stored GPT-6 turns: 10, all true re-narrations of the previous beat's close by hand; in gpt-4.1-mini's own play the owner's café terrace (the switch into a chapter's first step, which saw only the switch's summary) is caught.
- **For the run, before paying (the coordinator's reading):** arm C's own deterministic checks sit at their ceiling on production (1 of 82 turns reused), so under the stop rule they cannot move; its signal would be the judged "concrete progress" (a reading, uncalibrated) and a hand read. Arm O's per-chapter lever part touches 3 cases; its option-design part is where it can move (27% and 20% with noise ±3 and ±9).
- **Dry run** (`--prompt-state adopted2`): 352 jobs, est $1.29 of the $1.40 cap ($0.32 an arm), at least 34 min; the form gate measured $0.0033 a turn, about $1.16. The turn judge would add about $0.08 measured, but its pre-run estimate (about $0.31 for 328 judged turns) fits after the turns only in parts (`--cases`). A fix-and-retest fits only narrowed to the affected cases (the 32 rolled steps once, about $0.11).
- **Harness:** `stageInterleavesArms`/`interleaved` (jobPlan.ts) orders a stage's jobs sample by sample, case by case, the arms of one case together; only this stage. `reusedFromPrevious` is exported for hand reads.
- **Spend:** $0.

## Options and continuity, verification fixes (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **Arm O's lever rule is back to the coordinator's design (finding 1, important):** the build had also gated a second sacrifice on today's rate. Verified: under that gate the strong-reason clause could bind in production only at step 4 of a four-step chapter whose step 1 offered a lever. In a two- or three-step chapter the rate refuses a lever for two challenge turns after any, so the owner's "several sacrifices can sometimes make sense, but should have a strong justification starting at the second one" could never be offered there. On this stage, where every chapter has three steps, it bound only on `cont-7492b211-t2-o0..o2`. Their played step typed its challenge options as exploration, a state production never stores: `repairOptions` retypes them to challenge, which makes the step a lever turn and today's line "none this turn". (`synth-7492b211-t2-pregeneration_1_player1_1-noimg` is that turn as production would hold it, and there today's line gives none.) Now `chapterLeverRule`: a reward as today's rate allows and at most one a chapter; the chapter's first sacrifice as today's rate allows; from its second on, the chapter's count and not the rate. My call on the reward: I kept it under the rate. The design caps it per chapter and says nothing about lifting it, and lifting it would have loosened 3 more stored steps (`cont-8988006e-t5`).
- **What the rule reaches on the stored turns** (a temp probe, deleted; no calls): the line differs from today's on 24 of the 32 rolled steps.
  - The 3 `7492b211-t2` cases: production's line "one fits" on the unreachable state; arm O's line the strong-reason clause with "A reward fits", the same sacrifice clause a repaired history would get.
  - 21 steps where today's line gives none after a chapter sacrifice: production "none this turn"; arm O a second sacrifice only for a strong reason, and "No reward this turn." 10 come after one sacrifice (`cont-6edd813c-t2`, `cont-8988006e-t2`, `cont-8988006e-t6`, `synth-7492b211-t2`), a state production reaches. 11 come after two (`cont-6edd813c-t3`, `cont-8988006e-t3`, `cont-8988006e-t7`, `synth-8988006e-t3`, `synth-8988006e-t7`), which production reaches only past its own line and arm O under its own.
  - Arm O's request grows 516-530 characters on those 24 steps against 323 elsewhere, about 50 tokens more. The dry run reads as before: 352 jobs, est $1.29, turnO $0.322.
- **For the reading (the coordinator's call):** on the 21 steps arm O is looser than production by design, so there its lever half can hold level with production or lose, but not gain. Production's line gives none there, and only 2 of production's 7 second sacrifices sit on reachable states (`cont-8988006e-t2`, against its own line). The other 5 sit on the 3 artifact cases, where production's line allowed one, so a drop in second sacrifices for arm O would again ride on the artifact. Read the lever shares with and without `cont-7492b211-t2`.
  - `leverFollowsRateLine` fails wherever arm O offers a second sacrifice its line allows, by design.
  - The new `leverFollowsChapterLine` reads the arm's own line: no sacrifice where it gives none, no reward where it gives none, reported only where it forbids one.
  - The new `unreasonedSecondSacrificeShare` (of challenge sets) is the owner's criterion once a reasoned second sacrifice is allowed.
  - Whether arm O has to move therefore rests on its option-design half (`primaryStatsDistinct` 27% ±3, `sameStatsOnlyRiskShare` 20% ±9).
  - To return to the build's gate after all: `rateFits` would gate `sacrifice` when `sacrifices > 0` in `chapterLeverRule`, plus its tests. The stage would then read the lever half on no state production reaches.
- **Baselines of the two additions** (`--check-baselines`, the same stored replies): `leverFollowsChapterLine` production (adopted1) 96% ±0 of 48, today's form 81% ±8 of 96, gpt-4.1-mini 88% of 48; `unreasonedSecondSacrificeShare` production 11% ±9 (all 7 second sacrifices), today's form 41% ±3 (of 47%), gpt-4.1-mini 52% ±2 (of 53%).
- **The strong-reason wording (finding 2):** "so offer another only if the scene gives a strong reason to pay again" followed "which the player didn't take" on 2 of the 3 cases that sent it (and would on 13 of the 24 now: 8 "didn't take", 5 "took none of them"). Now "so offer another sacrifice only if the scene gives a strong reason for it, and make that reason clear in the option's text", which holds whether or not the player took one. A test pins every form, and that none says "again".
- **The request-level guard (finding 3):** arm O's request tests used histories with no lever, so nothing exercised the lever-line swap in `optionEdits`. Two new tests:
  - A three-step chapter's second step after a sacrifice, where the two lines differ. Arms O and OC carry the chapter's line and not today's; production and arm C carry today's; arm O with its edits undone equals the base.
  - Every frozen rolled step: arm O's request carries its chapter line once, and the lines differ on cases other than `7492b211-t2`.
  - Checked by removing the swap: both new tests fail and the rest pass.
- **Spend:** $0.

## Options and continuity, the run (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **Arm O (`turnO`) against production's form (stop rule):**
  - Own checks: main stats distinct 17.2% → 39.1% of challenge sets (p 0.005), main stats per set 1.67 → 2.16 (5.0 SE): moved. Where: the café stories' 11 rolled steps (11 of 22 sets → 21 of 22); on the Novi Reg stories, whose two skills are entries of one list stat (`skills_traits`), 0 of 42 → 4 of 42. Sets that only risk tells apart about 14% → 8%, within the noise. Rewards 7 → 2 sets (beyond the noise, not moved; 3 of production's 7 broke its own "none" line); second sacrifices 8 → 7, none moved; arm O's own line followed 91.7% → 95.8%.
  - Wrong way: same-first-word share 8.5% → 22.0% (p 0.014), the options naming their stat ("Use your Technical skill to…" / "Use your Creative skill to…": on the Novi Reg stories 14 sets against production's 5, 13 of arm O's 42 there with two options naming Technical or Creative against 3); negative base on bonus options 48.4% → 40.0% (p 0.081). The second follows from the design: arm O puts a bonus on the sensible option too (bonus options 1.74 → 1.93 a turn), while the negative-base count holds (0.84 → 0.77, 0.6 SE). The balance simulation reads level odds (favorable chance, every option, 41.8% → 42.3%), but a strength option has the best chance outright in 88% of sets against 77%, the thing B6's scale is there to temper.

  - Validity: 85 of 88 on the first attempt (FAIL by the gate's floor), all three cut on switch turns whose request is production's byte for byte (prompt hash `25faaf…` on `cont-8988006e-t4-o1`, `fc35bf…` on `-o2`); production cut 1 of 2 on the same request beside it. Not arm O's text. 88 of 88 within retries.
- **Arm C (`turnC`):** nothing moved either way. Its own checks sit at production's ceiling (no reused sentence or opening, 82 of 82 on both); judged concrete progress 86% → 92% within the 15-point noise, first paragraph narrates the choice 99% → 98%, step left open level. Beyond the noise, not moved: one of six endings wrote options (100% → 83.3%, p 0.5), sentences 97.7% → 93.2% (p 0.139). Hand read of the 12 chapter first steps per arm (the one input arm C adds): no arm re-narrates; arm C's openings pick up the switch's chosen action a little more often, production's narrate the choice too. 87 of 88, pass.
- **Arm OC (`turnOC`):** main stats distinct 17.2% → 48.4% (p < 0.001), its own line followed 100%, knownIds up (p 0.06). Wrong way: reward share of levers 41.2% → 6.3% (p 0.024), reward sets 0.08 → 0.01 a turn (2.2 SE); against arm O, paragraphs opening with "You" 23.7% → 30.5% (p 0.015). Second sacrifices on the reachable states (without `cont-7492b211-t2`) 2 → 7 of 58 (p 0.08, a hand count; the stop rule over all cases reads 12.5% → 15.6%, not moved), none with a stated reason. Validity 85 of 88 (FAIL by the floor): cuts on arm C's switch and ending requests on story 8988006e's turn 4 (`turnC` valid on the same requests).
- **Waits** (turn alone, p95; switch turns and chapter openings "turn only", the final check's planner p95s 6.9 s and 19.2 s to add by hand, summed reads high): chapter steps production 46.8 s (over 45 s itself at 8.9-9.2 ms a token), O 51.5, C 49.0, OC 58.9 (both OC samples 9-13 s over production's beside them); chapter openings production 45.1, O 41.5, C 47.7 (+2.6 and +3.3 s by sample, within production's own 3.8 s between samples), OC 53.3 (+8 s in both samples: slower than production, so OC fails the opening rule); switch turns 103-117 s in every arm, production included, from the runaways re-sent; endings 31-35 s, OC 98 s (one cut). The retest ran at 7.2 ms a token, so its waits (chapter steps 44.6 s) don't compare; its tokens are +1 to +3%.

Per 1-player story with pregeneration, billed: $0.284, $0.306, $0.293, $0.313.

On its 21 cases: same first word 0 of 21 (arm O 14 of 42, p 0.001; production 5 of 42, lower p 0.12), two options naming Technical or Creative 0 of 21 (arm O 13 of 42, production 3 of 42); against production nothing moved, and the judged checks read first paragraph 21 of 21, concrete progress 21 of 21, step left open 20 of 21. What it can't show: the gain, since these cases held almost none of it (main stats distinct 0 of 21, production 0 of 42, arm O 4 of 42). The negative-base share 50.0% → 44.8% (not moved) remains.

OC: rewards the wrong way and slower openings. To adopt arm O with the fix: a small paid check of `turnOb` on the café stories' 11 rolled steps ×2 (22 turns, about $0.08 read against production's records of this run; about $0.15 with production beside it for the waits), over this stage's $0.009 left, and the coordinator's call on the negative-base share.

Each costs the player about 70 s before the retry. Worth a look at what that state asks.

## Version O2, the build (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What was built, no calls, $0:** `turnO2` (`o2` in `turnOptionsContinuity.ts`), in arm O's place on production's single-player turn form: O's stat variety with the lever option left out of the bonus count, O's retest sentence, B6's negative base said to hold, and a lever line that invites a reward until the chapter has offered one (sacrifices as arm O's `chapterLeverRule`). Its readings (`mainStatsDistinctLeverApart`, `leverFollowsO2Line`, `rewardWhereInvitedShare`), a stage `options-o2` (cap $0.70, reason recorded) under a new prompt state `adopted3`, interleaved, on `OPTIONS_O2_CASES`. Test-first; production's request byte for byte wherever O2 doesn't apply, and O2's edits undone give it back on every frozen single-player turn.
- **Why O and OC cut rewards (read from the run's 64 rolled sets an arm, a temp probe, deleted):**
  - Arm O's stats line. "At most one has no stat bonus" left no room for a bare sensible option beside a bare lever: production had 4 sets with two bonus-less options, all lever sets; arm O had none. 6 of production's 7 reward options carry no bonus (a reward turns aside from the goal). So the reward was squeezed out, or the sensible option took a bonus at base 0 (normal options at base 0 or above carrying a bonus: 81 → 102). That second effect is the negative-base drop. An arm O plan says it outright: "The three normal approaches can draw on distinct current bonuses without spending a resource or diverting from planning." (Sacrifices carrying a bonus: production 5 of 10, O 4 of 11, so not O's own.)
  - Arm O's lever line. Where today's rate gives none after a chapter sacrifice, O's line ends "No reward this turn.". Production's 3 rewards on `cont-6edd813c-t2` (s1 of each case) broke its own "none this turn". Arm O's explicit sentence stopped them ("the instructions call for no reward this turn", an OC plan). So 3 of the 5 missing rewards were rule-breaks the explicit sentence closed.
  - Where a lever fit (10 turns, `cont-7492b211-t2` left out), rewards and sacrifices were: production 4 and 1, O 2 and 2, OC 1 and 5, C 4 and 4. OC's further drop fits O's cause. Ten turns can't separate C's share of it.
- **How O2 avoids them (my wording calls):**
  - "The options also draw on different stats" instead of "Each option also draws…" (a reward draws on none).
  - "At most one option that is neither a sacrifice nor a reward has no stat bonus". The brief's "at most one with no stat bonus" is kept for the normal options.
  - "A sacrifice or reward option needs no stat bonus: what it spends or gains already sets it apart." This also answers "Risk alone never tells two options apart" for a reward, whose base is -30.
  - The line invites a reward first and never says "No reward" while the chapter has offered none.
  - The negative base, my call on the brief's "restore B6's trade-off wherever O weakened it": "Drawing on different stats doesn't change basePoints: the option that plays to the character's strength still takes -5 to -15, even when every option earns a bonus." I didn't write "every option with a bonus pays a negative base": B6's +5 for "one that uses something the story has established (… an ally's promise)" can carry a Contacts bonus, so that would contradict the scale. The main fix for the negative base is structural: a bare sensible option is allowed again beside a lever.
- **The reward invitation, the brief's call made concrete:** a reward is invited wherever the chapter has offered none, whatever today's rate says. The build's earlier "I kept it under the rate" is superseded by the brief ("where a stat's rules allow one and the chapter hasn't offered one"). On the stored steps this changes the line on all 32 rolled steps (no stored chapter offered a reward before its turn), 21 of them where production says "none this turn". The risk to read in the run: the lever share can rise, since a reward is no longer spaced from a sacrifice by the rate. The sacrifice half is arm O's rule unchanged (a test holds it).
- **Read end to end** (the O2 prompt, the schema unchanged and byte for byte production's):
  - a chapter's first step with the rate at none (`cont-8988006e-t5-o0`: "a reward fits … No sacrifice this turn.");
  - a step after a taken sacrifice (`cont-6edd813c-t2-o1`: invitation plus the strong-reason clause);
  - a step where a reward fits and nothing was offered (`cont-2ee343b6-t2-o0`, `cont-checkpoi-t1-o0`: "a reward fits this turn if a stat allows it, and so does a sacrifice.");
  - a last step after a reward, built in memory from `cont-8988006e-t7-o1` with the chapter's sacrifices made rewards: today's "none this turn" (reward closed, no first sacrifice by the rate).
  - A switch turn and the ending are production's byte for byte (tests).
  - Checked against B6's three ways, its weak and good examples (the good example's sacrifice names the stat it pays, which the retest sentence allows: "its bonus"), the modifiers line (no bonus for the stat a lever spends or gains: O's count pressed a lever toward a bonus, O2's says it needs none), the base-point field, NO_DOUBLE_SACRIFICE and the 0-or-1 lever rule. Nothing contradicts.
- **Checks:**
  - `mainStatsDistinctLeverApart` reads O2's own rule. `primaryStatsDistinct` stays the owner's variety reading, and it fails O2's allowed [bare, strength, bare lever] shape, so read both.
  - `leverFollowsO2Line` is reported only where the line forbids a lever.
  - `rewardWhereInvitedShare` covers the rolled sets whose chapter offered no reward yet.
  - Baselines on the run's replies: `mainStatsDistinctLeverApart` production 23% ±3, O 39%, OC 48%; `rewardWhereInvitedShare` production 11% ±3 (7 of 64), C 9%, O 3%, OC 2%; `leverFollowsO2Line` binds only on `cont-8988006e-t5` (6 sets).
  - `check-baselines.md` was re-rendered with them.
- **The stage, my calls:**
  - Only the 32 stored rolled steps, since O2 sends production's request byte for byte everywhere else.
  - Production runs beside O2 at samples 1 and 2 under `adopted3`, so the waits compare in the same hour, as the report's "about $0.15 with production beside it" advised.
  - Second references are `turnO` and `turnOb` (stored `adopted2`), and O2 is priced from `turnOb`.
- **Dry run** (`--prompt-state adopted3`): 128 jobs, est $0.48 of $0.70 (`adopted` 64, $0.234; `turnO2` 64, $0.245), at least 13 min; that leaves room for the judge (about $0.03) and one fix and retest (O2 once on the 32, about $0.11).
- **Spend:** $0; the ledger $35.41 of $40.

## Version O2, verification fix (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **Finding (minor, verified):** O2 invites a reward on the 21 rolled stored steps where today's rate gives none after a chapter sacrifice, and there `leverFollowsRateLine` (no lever where today's line gives none) and `leverFollowsChapterLine` (arm O's `chapterLeverRule`, whose reward needs the rate) both forbid one. Both run on every arm and `results.md` reads every check against production, so each invited reward O2 offers there reads as a wrong-way move with no by-design note. Only arm O's reasoned second sacrifice had one.
- **Fix, documentation only (my call):** one sentence in `feedbackChecks`' comment and in the context doc's O2 readings: O2 fails those two checks by design wherever it offers a reward its line invites and today's rate gives none, so its run reads `leverFollowsO2Line`, `atMostOneRewardPerChapter` and `rewardWhereInvitedShare` in their place. No code change: the checks don't know the arm, and splitting them would move production's baselines (its 3 rewards on `cont-6edd813c-t2` broke its own line, which `leverFollowsRateLine` is there to see). The O2 report reads the two with this note.
- **Spend:** $0.

## Version O2, the run (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What ran:** requests read end to end first (a temp probe, deleted: a first step with the rate at none `cont-8988006e-t5-o0`, a step after a taken sacrifice `cont-6edd813c-t2-o1`, a step where a reward fits `cont-2ee343b6-t2-o0`, the schema; nothing contradicts), a 4-job smoke, then production's `adopted` and `turnO2` ×2 on the 32 stored rolled chapter steps under `adopted3`, interleaved (124 open jobs as the dry run counted; 04:43-04:59 UTC, 7.7-8.2 ms a token), 124 of 124 valid on the first attempt, then the judged checks on all 128 turns ($0.031).
- **Not the 44 turns (my call, against the brief's "44"):** O2 sends production's request byte for byte on the other 12 stored single-player turns (a test holds it), so running them would have bought only more production samples and the known turn-4 runaways, at about $0.10, and left no room for the fix and retest.
- **Spend:** $0.44 for the turns, $0.031 for the judge; stage $0.47 of $0.70; the ledger $35.88 of $40.
- **O2 against production (stop rule, 64 pairs):**
  - Its own checks moved: `mainStatsDistinctLeverApart` 22.2% → 60.9% (p < 0.001), `primaryStatsDistinct` 17.5% → 56.3% (p < 0.001; café 11 of 22 → 19 of 22, Novi Reg 0 of 41 → 17 of 42, arm O's 4), `sameStatsOnlyRiskShare` 15.9% → 1.6% (p 0.004), odds within 5 points 22.2% → 3.1% (p 0.001). Rewards: `rewardWhereInvitedShare` 9.4% → 23.4% (p 0.027; 6 → 15 sets), most where the rate gives none (`cont-8988006e-t5` 1 → 4 of 6, `cont-6edd813c-t2` 3 → 4).
  - Arm O's wrong-way moves are gone: negative base on bonus options 48.7% → 53.7% (not moved; against arm O 40.0% → 53.7%, p 0.012), same first word 0 sets (arm O 26.6%), strength option best outright 71% → 69% in the balance simulation (arm O's 88%).
  - **Wrong way: sacrifices.** Lever sets 31.7% → 76.6% (p < 0.001), `secondSacrificeShare` 17.5% → 43.8% (p 0.001), `unreasonedSecondSacrificeShare` 12.7% → 39.1% (p < 0.001); sacrifice sets 14 → 34 of 64. Where: the 21 steps after a chapter sacrifice (42 sets without `cont-7492b211-t2`): O2 22 second sacrifices, production 5 (against its own "none"); café 1, Novi Reg 21, nearly all "Spend 20 Resource Credits on …" (a token, an audit, transit vouchers). By hand none of the 22 gives a scene reason for paying again (the heuristic's two are "before submitting"/"before making it public"); arm O's clause gave 0 in 7 too. The route, by my read: the stats line's "A sacrifice or reward option needs no stat bonus" makes a bare lever the easy third option where the stats are few (Novi Reg's skills sit in one list stat), and after a sacrifice the strong-reason clause reads as leave.
  - By design (the verification fix above): `leverFollowsRateLine` 81.3% → 27.1%, `leverFollowsChapterLine` 91.7% → 72.9%. `leverFollowsO2Line` 100% (binds on `cont-8988006e-t5` only).
  - Judged: step left open 94% → 92%, concrete progress 88% → 84%, first paragraph 97% → 97%, all within the noise.
  - Waits (turn alone; openings "turn only"): chapter steps p95 39.4 → 44.1 s (by sample 41.6 → 49.0, 39.2 → 40.8; production's samples 2.4 s apart), openings 38.5 → 46.8 s (38.2 → 46.8, 38.5 → 43.5; production's 0.3 s apart; 5 turns a sample). Reasoning tokens +14% (1,754 → 1,992), p50 +2.2 s. Both within the allowances (45 s, 60 s), but the openings read slower in both samples.
  - Cost: $0.0034 → $0.0036 a turn (+5%), $0.287 → $0.302 per 1-player story with pregeneration.
- **Verdict: O2 does not pass.** Its variety and reward gains are real and O's wrong-way moves are gone, but sacrifices move the wrong way (the owner's "a strong justification starting at the second one"), and the openings read slower. Not adopted.

## Version O2's fix-and-retest, the build (implementer, 2026-09-30)

No sacrifice this turn."). Everything else is O2's request byte for byte (a test holds it).
